### [54] [The Swiss Roll Metamaterial Revisited](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02758)
*Zachary Fritts,Anthony Grbic*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically models Swiss roll metamaterial as flux-coupled waveguides with coupled inductors, derives a closed-form solution for its circuit model, and provides a general method for effective permeability of uniaxial anisotropic metamaterials with axially invariant conducting inclusions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop an analytical model for Swiss roll metamaterials that accurately predicts their electromagnetic response, including higher-order resonances and lossy conductor effects.

Method: The authors model each unit cell as a flux-coupled waveguide terminated in coupled inductors, derive an equivalent circuit model, and solve a matrix equation of arbitrarily large dimensions to obtain a closed-form solution. They also propose a general method for modeling effective permeability of uniaxial anisotropic metamaterials.

Result: The equivalent circuit model accurately predicts the metamaterial's response, including higher-order resonances, even with lossy finite-thickness conductors.

Conclusion: The analytical model provides accurate predictions for Swiss roll metamaterials and offers a general approach for modeling uniaxial anisotropic metamaterials with axially invariant conducting inclusions.

Abstract: In this work, we analytically model the Swiss roll metamaterial medium, showing that each unit cell can be thought of as a flux-coupled waveguide terminated in coupled inductors. The equivalent circuit model of the metamaterial accurately predicts its response, including its higher order resonances, even when made of a lossy, finite-thickness conductor. A closed-form solution for the response of this circuit model is then derived by solving a matrix equation of arbitrarily large dimensions. Along the way, we provide a general method for modeling the effective permeability of uniaxial, anisotropic metamaterials formed from axially invariant conducting inclusions.

</details>


### [55] [Synergistic enhancement of Bi2Te3 Sb2Te3 PMMA thermoelectric generators via dithiol assisted conductivity and FEM based geometry optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02793)
*Bejan Hamawandi,Jose F. Serrano Claumarchirant,Adem B. Ergul,Kaspars Pudzs,Inga Pudza,Parva Parsa,Metehan Kirsanli,Oskars Bitmets,Muhammet S. Toprak*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: The paper reports hybrid thermoelectric inks based on Sb₂Te₃ and Bi₂Te₃ nanoparticles with PMMA and HDT binder, achieving 80% nanoparticle content as optimal. FEM-optimized flexible hTEG delivers 950 nW output and 40.37 nW cm⁻² power density under 30 K gradient, outperforming prior polymer-based chalcogenide hTEGs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the need for energy-efficient, reliable, and scalable synthetic routes for fabricating thermoelectric materials and devices, enabling broader adoption of waste-heat-to-electricity conversion technology.

Method: Formulation of hTE inks using nanostructured Sb₂Te₃ and Bi₂Te₃ with PMMA polymer matrix and HDT binder; percolation studies for optimal nanoparticle loading (80% found optimal); finite element modelling (FEM) to optimize device geometry (cross-sectional area ratio of p- and n-legs); fabrication of flexible hTEG.

Result: Flexible hTEG achieved 950 nW output power and 40.37 nW cm⁻² power density under a 30 K temperature gradient, significantly outperforming previously reported polymer-based flexible hTEGs with chalcogenides.

Conclusion: A sustainable and effective strategy combining ink formulation, composition optimization, and simulation-guided device design successfully produces high-performance hybrid thermoelectric devices.

Abstract: In recent decades, thermoelectric (TE) materials have proven to be a complementary source of renewable energy, as they can directly convert waste heat into electrical energy. Energy-efficient, reliable, and scalable synthetic routes for the fabrication of TE materials and their processing into functional devices via low-energy and low-waste routes are necessary for the broader adoption of these materials in various applications. In this work, we report the formulation of hybrid thermoelectric (hTE) inks based on nanostructured Sb2Te3 and Bi2Te3, using PMMA as the polymer matrix and hexanedithiol (HDT) as the binder. Percolation studies were conducted to determine the optimal film composition, with an 80% nanoparticle content yielding the highest TE performance. Finite element modelling (FEM) was employed to optimize the device geometry, including the cross-sectional area ratio of p- and n-type legs, to maximize power output. Based on these results, a flexible hTEG was fabricated using the optimized ink composition. The device exhibited an output power of 950 nW and a Power Output Density (PoD) of 40.37 nW cm-2 under a 30 K temperature gradient, significantly outperforming previously reported polymer-based flexible hTEGs incorporating chalcogenides. This study presents a sustainable and effective strategy for developing high-performance hybrid thermoelectric devices through ink formulation, composition optimization, and simulation-guided device design.

</details>


### [56] [Complex Refractive Index Determination via Microspectroscopy Through Magnifying Optics: Challenges and Opportunities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03077)
*Julian Schwarz,Johannes Bauer,Mathias Rommel,Andreas Hutzler*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: A fast, non-destructive microspectroscopic method is presented for extracting complex refractive indices from reflectance/transmittance spectra of micrometer-scale samples, accounting for numerical aperture effects without dispersion models. Validated on glasses, 4H-SiC, SixNy films, graphite, and MoO3, it shows excellent agreement with literature and is proposed as an alternative to spectroscopic ellipsometry, though limited for out-of-plane indices in graphite.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate knowledge of complex refractive indices is essential for designing and optimizing optoelectronic devices; existing methods lack speed, non-destructiveness, or applicability to micrometer-scale samples.

Method: Microspectroscopy combining an optical microscope and spectrometer; a numerical formalism accounting for numerical aperture effects enables precise refractive index retrieval without dispersion models.

Result: Excellent agreement with literature values for transparent glasses, 4H-SiC, SixNy thin films, exfoliated graphite, and MoO3; graphite case reveals limited sensitivity for out-of-plane refractive indices.

Conclusion: The approach is a methodologically sound, fast, non-destructive alternative to spectroscopic ellipsometry; possible extensions toward retrieving full anisotropic refractive index tensors are discussed.

Abstract: For the design and optimization of optoelectronic devices, accurate knowledge of the complex refractive indices of the constituent materials is essential. Herein, we present a fast and non-destructive approach for the extraction of the refractive indices from reflectance and transmittance spectra of samples with lateral dimensions down to the micrometer scale. Microspectroscopy, based on the combination of a standard optical microscope and a spectrometer, enables the assessment of the optical response of multilayer stacks using high-magnification optics with correspondingly large numerical apertures. Employing a numerical formalism explicitly accounting for the influence of the numerical aperture, allows for precise retrieval of the refractive index without resorting to dispersion models. We demonstrate the applicability of the proposed method for large-area, homogeneous, optically incoherent samples such as transparent glasses and absorbing 4H-SiC, for a SixNy thin film on glass substrate, and for mechanically exfoliated flakes of highly oriented pyrolytic graphite and MoO3, as representatives of uniaxial and biaxial optical anisotropy. While the results prove excellent agreement with values reported in literature, the case of graphite highlights the limitation for probing the out-of-plane refractive indices due to reduced sensitivity. Finally, we discuss possible extensions towards retrieving the full anisotropic tensor of the refractive index, establishing the proposed approach as a methodologically sound alternative to spectroscopic ellipsometry.

</details>


### [57] [Efficient Analysis of Carrier Transport and TM-TE Emission in AlGaN UVC LEDs via Multi-band Localization Landscape Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03753)
*Yu-Ming Chang,Ping-Jie Zhuang,Marcel Filoche,Claude Weisbuch,James S. Speck,Yuh-Renn Wu*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes a multiband Localization Landscape (LL) model as a computationally efficient alternative to conventional k.p methods for simulating optical and electrical properties of high-Al-content AlGaN UVC LEDs (220-250 nm), accurately reproducing emission/absorption spectra and polarization ratios while significantly reducing simulation time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: AlGaN-based UVC LEDs with high Al content (>60%) suffer from poor hole confinement and strain-induced |Z>-band dominance, causing increased TM emission and reduced EQE. Conventional k.p models are accurate but computationally expensive, motivating the need for a faster yet reliable simulation approach.

Method: The authors apply the multiband Localization Landscape (LL) model incorporating strain effects, combined with the Wigner-Weyl formalism, as an alternative that replaces the eigenvalue problem to efficiently capture quantum effects and carrier localization in 3D.

Result: The LL model reproduces emission and absorption spectra trends similar to 3D k.p calculations with significantly reduced simulation time. The polarization ratio agrees well with published experimental results across a wide spectral range. Electrical characteristics such as band structure, polarization switching, and carrier confinement under alloy fluctuations and strain are also analyzed.

Conclusion: The multiband LL-based approach provides a fast and reliable solution for understanding and optimizing UVC LED performance, offering a practical alternative to computationally expensive conventional methods.

Abstract: AlGaN-based UVC LEDs (220-250 nm) suffer from poor hole confinement and strain-induced |Z>-band dominance at high Al content (>60%), leading to increased TM emission and reduced external quantum efficiency (EQE). While conventional k.p models combined with Schrodinger, Poisson, and drift-diffusion solvers are widely used to study optical transitions, they are computationally expensive. In this work, we apply the multiband Localization Landscape (LL) model, including the effect of strain, as an alternative that replaces the eigenvalue problem to efficiently capture quantum effects and carrier localization. Using the 3D multi-band LL model with the Wigner-Weyl formalism, we reproduce emission and absorption spectra trends similar to the results in 3D k.p calculations, but with significantly reduced simulation time. The polarization ratio also agrees well with published experimental results across a wide spectral range. Furthermore, we analyze electrical characteristics such as band structure, polarization switching, and carrier confinement under alloy fluctuations and strain. This multi-band LL-based approach provides a fast and reliable solution for understanding and optimizing UVC LED performance.

</details>


### [58] [Enhanced LIBS Emission Using Laser Beam Splitting: Interacting Multi-Plume Plasma Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04229)
*Girum Abebe Beyene,Inam Mirza,Jijil JJ Nivas,Oleksandr Gatsa,Nadezhda M. Bulgakova,Alexander V. Bulgakov*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: Using a diffractive optical element to transform a Gaussian beam into a 2x2 array enhances LIBS emission intensity by ~9× for Si and ~3× for Cu due to plume-plume interactions, offering a simple signal enhancement method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Beam shaping effects in LIBS remain insufficiently quantified, especially for compact instrumentation.

Method: A DOE transforms a Gaussian beam into a 2x2 array for multi-spot ablation; plasma evolution from Cu and Si targets is studied in vacuum with time-resolved imaging and OES using a Nd:YAG laser (1064 nm, 5 ns, 10 J/cm²).

Result: Multi-spot configuration yields ~9× (Si) and ~3× (Cu) emission enhancement over single-spot irradiation.

Conclusion: DOE-based beam shaping is an effective, simple approach to increase LIBS signal without additional system complexity.

Abstract: The optical emission in laser-induced breakdown spectroscopy (LIBS) is governed by the spatial intensity distribution of the incident laser beam, which influences plasma formation and evolution. Beam shaping therefore offers a route to control plasma dynamics and emission yield; however, its effects in LIBS remain insufficiently quantified, particularly under conditions relevant to compact instrumentation. In this work, a diffractive optical element (DOE) is used to transform a Gaussian beam into a 2x2 array, producing simultaneously expanding, co-propagating ablation plumes that interact during expansion. Plasma evolution from Cu and Si targets is investigated in vacuum using a Nd:YAG laser (1064 nm, 5 ns, 10 J/cm2), combining time-resolved imaging with optical emission spectroscopy. The multi-spot configuration results in enhanced emission intensity compared to single-spot irradiation, with increases of ~9 for Si and ~3 for Cu. The observed enhancement is attributed to plume-plume interaction effects that modify plasma density and emission characteristics. These results demonstrate that DOE-based beam shaping provides an effective and technically simple approach to increasing the LIBS signal without additional system complexity.

</details>


### [59] [Experimental Realization of Type-II Quadrupole Topological Insulator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05049)
*Yuan Liu,Yangkai Liu,Cheng Lin,Jiao Shen,Yifan Zhu,Haiyan Fan,Hui Zhang*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: The authors experimentally realize type-II quadrupole topological insulators in elastic wave platforms by using an optimized Householder tridiagonalization mapping that simplifies complex 2D lattices into 1D chains with only negative-real-valued nearest-neighbor hopping terms, enabling practical fabrication and verification of type-II QTI, type-I QTI, and trivial phases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Type-II QTIs exhibit unconventional topological behaviors but their experimental realization is challenging due to long-range and complex off-site hopping terms in their tight-binding model.

Method: An optimized Householder tridiagonalization (OHT) mapping reduces complex 2D lattices to 1D chains with only negative-real-valued nearest-neighbor hopping terms, implemented via simple aperiodic plate-beam chain structures in elastic wave platforms.

Result: Experimental verification of type-II QTI phase, type-I QTI phase, and trivial phase in elastic wave platforms.

Conclusion: The OHT approach provides a versatile route for experimentally exploring more complex and richer topological phenomena based on tight-binding models.

Abstract: The discovery of quadrupole topological insulators (QTIs) has spurred extensive research into higher-order topological phases. Recently proposed type-II QTIs exhibit unconventional topological behaviors with 1/2 edge polarization \operatorname{p}_x and zero edge polarization \operatorname{p}_y, due to the inequivalence between Wannier-band and edge-spectrum gap closures, yet their experimental realization remains challenging owing to the long-range and complex off-site hopping terms in their tight-binding model (TBM). Here, we circumvent this difficulty via an optimized Householder tridiagonalization (OHT) mapping that reduces the complex two-dimensional lattices to one-dimensional chains with only negative-real-valued nearest-neighbor hopping terms, greatly facilitating experimental sample fabrication. Using this strategy, we experimentally verify the type-II QTI phase, type-I QTI phase and trivial phase in elastic wave platforms via simple aperiodic plate-beam chain structures, where the plates reflect the on-site potential terms and beams correspond to the off-site hopping terms in the TBM. Our approach provides a versatile route for experimentally exploring more complex and richer topological phenomena based on TBM.

</details>


<div id='physics.comp-ph'></div>

# physics.comp-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [60] [CodeJeNN: A simple C++ neural network generator for physics applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02746)
*Jay Arcities,Pavel Popov,Eric J Ching,Kamal Viswanath,Ryan F Johnson*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: CodeJeNN auto-generates self-contained C++ code from trained Keras models for inference, eliminating Python dependencies and achieving speedups in physics applications like CFD without sacrificing accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Python-based ML libraries (e.g., Keras) create performance bottlenecks when integrated into high-performance C++ solvers; a bridge is needed to combine ML inference speedups with native C++ performance.

Method: CodeJeNN auto-generates self-contained C++ inference code from trained Keras models using minimal inlined functions, supporting a Keras-to-C++ workflow with specified architectures.

Result: Inference benchmarks against Keras (eager and JIT modes) and a CFD test case (viscosity modeling in hydrogen-air mixing layer) show speedups without accuracy loss.

Conclusion: CodeJeNN effectively bridges Python ML and C++ numerical solvers, enabling performant, dependency-free ML inference in high-performance computing contexts.

Abstract: Machine learning has shown speedups for numerical methods in physics applications, but integrating Python-based libraries into high-performance C++ solvers creates performance bottlenecks. We present CodeJeNN, which bridges this gap by auto-generating self-contained C++ code from trained Keras models for inference. This eliminates external dependencies through minimal inlined functions, allowing seamless integration into existing frameworks. We describe the Keras-to-C++ workflow, supported architectures, and limitations. CodeJeNN is demonstrated through inference benchmarks against Keras in eager and JIT modes and a CFD test case modeling viscosity in a hydrogen-air mixing layer, showing speedups without sacrificing accuracy.

</details>


### [61] [Restoring the uniform density limit in Perdew-Zunger self-interaction correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03997)
*Benjamin G. Janesko*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an alternative to the Perdew-Zunger self-interaction correction (PZ-SIC) orbital density ansatz that restores the uniform density limit, reduces orbital dependence and lobed density error, and demonstrates improved accuracy in a nonempirical locally scaled self-interaction-corrected GGA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: PZ-SIC makes approximate density functionals exact for one-electron densities but sacrifices exactness for uniform densities. The author aims to fix this limitation while also reducing errors from lobed one-electron densities.

Method: The author introduces an alternative to the orbital density ansatz used in PZ-SIC that restores the uniform density limit and eliminates the need to evaluate approximate density functionals on lobed one-electron densities, reducing orbital dependence and lobed density error.

Result: The alternative ansatz restores the uniform density limit, reduces orbital dependence and lobed density error, and is demonstrated in a broadly accurate nonempirical locally scaled self-interaction-corrected generalized gradient approximation (GGA).

Conclusion: The new ansatz improves upon PZ-SIC by restoring exactness for uniform densities while reducing errors associated with lobed one-electron densities, leading to a more accurate nonempirical self-interaction-corrected GGA.

Abstract: The Perdew-Zunger self-interaction correction (PZ-SIC) makes approximate density functionals exact for all one-electron densities, but sacrifices exactness for uniform densities. I show that an alternative to the orbital density ansatz employed in PZ-SIC restores the uniform density limit. The new ansatz also eliminates the need to evaluate approximate density functionals on lobed one-electron densities extracted from smooth many-electron densities, thereby reducing orbital dependence and lobed density error. I demonstrate the alternative ansatz in a broadly accurate nonempirical locally scaled self-interaction-corrected generalized gradient approximation.

</details>


### [62] [Hyper Boris integrators for kinetic plasma simulations and their connection to 3D rotation representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04465)
*S. Zenitani,T. N. Kato*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents novel high-accuracy particle integrators called hyper Boris integrators for nonrelativistic kinetic simulations, improving upon the classic Boris method, and discusses their connection to 3D rotation representations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Despite the Boris method's longstanding use and good accuracy in PIC simulations, there is a growing demand for even higher-accuracy numerical solvers for charged particle motion.

Method: The authors develop hyper Boris integrators, a family of high-accuracy particle integrators for nonrelativistic kinetic simulations, and analyze their relationship to 3D rotation representations.

Result: Novel hyper Boris integrators are introduced that achieve higher accuracy than the traditional Boris method.

Conclusion: The hyper Boris integrators provide improved accuracy for PIC simulations and are connected to 3D rotation formalisms, offering a pathway for more precise kinetic plasma simulations.

Abstract: Particle-in-cell (PIC) simulation is one of the most important research tools in theoretical plasma physics. To solve the motion of charged particles, the Boris method (a.k.a. the Boris integrator/pusher/solver) has been used for more than a half century. Although the Boris solver has good accuracy, the demand for high-accuracy numerical solvers has been increasing, and new integrators have been actively developed. In this contribution, we present novel high-accuracy particle integrators, the hyper Boris integrators, for nonrelativistic kinetic simulations. We further discuss their connection to 3D rotation representations.

</details>


### [63] [Accelerating Multi-scale Simulations of Nuclear Components via PCYS Interpolation Tables](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04556)
*Fabrizio Aguzzi,Martin S. Armoa,Cesar I. Pairetti,Cesar M. Venier,Alejandro E. Albanesi*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: A multi-scale framework using a 5D Interpolation Table (PCYS) with periodic recalibration and Taylor linearization is developed in Code_Aster to efficiently model irradiation creep and growth in Zr alloy nuclear components, achieving <1% error relative to direct VPSC-FEM while significantly reducing computational cost for engineering-scale simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Micromechanical crystal plasticity (VPSC) captures irradiation-induced anisotropic dimensional changes in Zr alloys but is computationally prohibitive for integration into macroscopic FEM solvers for engineering-scale components like spacer grids and fuel cladding.

Method: The framework employs a 5D Interpolation Table (IT) as a static surrogate for instantaneous viscoplastic responses, coupled with periodic recalibration and first-order Taylor series linearization to track microstructural drift from radiation damage, avoiding on-the-fly database updates.

Result: Material-level assessment shows relative errors below 1% for representative deformation paths. Structural simulations of a spacer grid demonstrate meaningful computational savings while preserving microstructural fidelity.

Conclusion: The PCYS interpolation approach overcomes the multi-scale computational penalty and shows potential for multiphysics structural assessments and safety margin evaluations of nuclear core internals over operational lifespans.

Abstract: Zirconium alloy core components in nuclear reactors, such as spacer grids and fuel cladding, undergo anisotropic dimensional changes driven by coupled irradiation creep and growth. While micromechanical crystal plasticity frameworks like the Viscoplastic Self-Consistent (VPSC) formulation capture these microstructurally driven phenomena, their integration into macroscopic Finite Element Method (FEM) solvers is computationally prohibitive for engineering-scale components. To bridge this gap, this work presents a multi-scale framework implemented within the open-source FEM solver Code_Aster. The developed interface uses a 5D Interpolation Table (IT) as a static material surrogate to govern instantaneous viscoplastic responses, coupled with a periodic recalibration and first-order Taylor series linearization scheme to track microstructural drift due to radiation damage without on-the-fly database updates. The predictive accuracy, numerical stability, and performance of this Polycrystal Yield Surface (PCYS) interpolation approach are benchmarked against VPSC-FEM simulations under continuous high-dose irradiation scenarios. Material-level assessments demonstrate that the linearization scheme bounds relative errors below 1% for representative deformation paths, maintaining structural compatibility. Furthermore, structural simulations of a spacer grid domain revealed meaningful computational savings, overcoming the multi-scale computational penalty while preserving microstructural fidelity. The proposed framework shows potential for multiphysics structural assessments and safety margin evaluations of core internals over operational lifespans.

</details>


### [64] [dpti: An Automated Thermodynamic Integration Workflow for Phase Diagram Calculations with Machine Learning Interatomic Potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05015)
*Fengbo Yuan,Xin Zhong,Donghao Zheng,Jinzhe Zeng,Linfeng Zhang,Han Wang,Yifan Li*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: dpti is an open-source Python package that automates thermodynamic integration workflows for phase diagram calculations using machine learning interatomic potentials, demonstrated on silica and ice systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: TI calculations with MLIPs are technically challenging due to the need for careful design of reversible integration paths and many related MD tasks for each phase and state point.

Method: dpti automates TI workflows by connecting reference systems with analytically known free energies to MLIP-described systems via reversible integration paths. It generates and runs MD tasks, computes free energy contributions, estimates errors, and propagates coexistence points into phase boundaries from JSON input files.

Result: Demonstrated on two Deep Potential model examples: a silica phase diagram (beta-quartz, coesite, melt) and the ice Ih-liquid water phase boundary.

Conclusion: dpti provides a useful automated tool for phase diagram calculations of materials modeled by MLIPs.

Abstract: Thermodynamic integration (TI) is a widely used approach for computing free energies and phase diagrams. However, TI calculations driven by machine learning interatomic potentials (MLIPs) remain technically challenging because they require careful design of reversible integration paths and many closely related molecular dynamics (MD) tasks for each phase and state point. To address these challenges, we present dpti, an open-source Python package that automates TI workflows for phase diagram calculations with MLIPs. dpti connects reference systems with analytically known free energies to MLIP-described atomic and molecular solids and liquids through reversible integration paths. Given JSON input files, dpti generates and runs the required MD tasks, computes free energy contributions, estimates errors, and propagates coexistence points into phase boundaries. We demonstrate the usage of dpti with two examples driven by Deep Potential models: a silica phase diagram involving beta-quartz, coesite, and melt, and the ice Ih-liquid water phase boundary. dpti provides a useful tool for automated phase diagram calculations of materials modeled by MLIPs.

</details>


### [65] [Watts per event: evaluating Sustainability of HEP Event Generators beyond the LHC era](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05018)
*Szabolcs Molnár,Gábor Bíró,Gábor Papp,Gergely Gábor Barnaföldi*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the sustainability of Monte Carlo event generators for post-LHC high-energy physics by benchmarking the HIJING++ heavy-ion generator with a containerized toolset (77rev/propripy). It analyzes CPU architecture performance and demonstrates that optimizing multithreading levels can reduce energy footprint and computational costs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The development, tuning and operation of Monte Carlo event generators beyond the LHC era require vast amounts of resources, raising sustainability concerns.

Method: The study uses a containerized set of tools (named 77rev/propripy) to benchmark the HIJING++ heavy-ion Monte Carlo event generator, analyzing the performance of various CPU architectures and optimizing multithreading levels.

Result: By choosing the level of multithreading properly, the cost of event generation can be optimized across different CPU architectures.

Conclusion: The presented approach can reduce the energy footprint of high-energy physics event generators, alleviating the ever-increasing computational challenges.

Abstract: The development, tuning and operation of Monte Carlo event generators beyond the LHC era require vast amount of resources. In this study we investigate the sustainability of these software with a containerized set of tools (named 77rev/propripy), by benchmarking the HIJING++ heavy-ion Monte Carlo event generator. We analyze the performance of various CPU architectures and show that by choosing the level of multithreading properly, the cost of event generation can be optimized. The presented approach can reduce the energy footprint of high-energy physics event generators and therefore alleviate the ever-increasing, ubiquitous computational challenges.

</details>

### [66] [Theoretical investigation of power absorbed by superconductors with Rashba spin-orbit coupling in the presence external time-dependent perturbation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02812)
*Reza Afzali,Bahman Takrim,Amir Hossein Asghari*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This paper theoretically investigates the absorbed power in superconductors under time-dependent perturbations via Bogoliubov quasiparticle transitions, focusing on how the superconducting coherence factor, gap, excitation spectrum, and external frequency affect absorption. Applied to a Hubbard-model superconductor with correlated hopping and Rashba spin-orbit coupling, numerical calculations show that absorption is strongly influenced by the superconducting gap, quasiparticle spectrum, and spin-orbit coupling, highlighting the importance of hopping processes, interaction strength, and spin-orbit coupling in ultrasonic and electromagnetic absorption.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how absorbed power in superconductors driven by time-dependent perturbations is governed by transitions between Bogoliubov quasiparticle states, particularly examining the role of the superconducting coherence factor and how various material parameters (interaction strength, correlated hopping, Rashba spin-orbit coupling) affect the absorption response.

Method: The study derives the transition matrix element containing the superconducting coherence factor for absorption between Bogoliubov quasiparticle states, then applies the formalism to a Hubbard-model superconductor that includes correlated hopping and Rashba spin-orbit coupling. Numerical calculations are performed to obtain quasiparticle energies, density of states, chemical potential, and temperature-dependent superconducting gap, from which the normalized absorbed power is evaluated across different parameter values.

Result: The absorption response is strongly affected by the superconducting gap and changes in the quasiparticle spectrum. Rashba spin-orbit coupling modifies the density of states and consequently alters the absorption. Different values of temperature, interaction strength, correlated hopping parameter, and spin-orbit coupling strength all significantly influence the normalized absorbed power.

Conclusion: Hopping processes, interaction strength, and spin-orbit coupling play important roles in the ultrasonic and electromagnetic absorption of superconducting systems, demonstrating that these parameters must be considered when analyzing absorption in superconductors.

Abstract: The absorbed power in superconductors driven by a time-dependent external perturbation is investigated through transitions between Bogoliubov quasiparticle states. The transition matrix element contains the superconducting coherence factor, which determines the role of the gap, excitation spectrum, and external frequency in the absorption process. The formulation is applied to a Hubbard-model superconductor including correlated hopping and Rashba spin-orbit coupling. In this model, numerical calculations are used to obtain the quasiparticle energies, density of states, chemical potential, and temperature-dependent superconducting gap. The normalized absorbed power is then evaluated for different values of temperature, interaction strength, correlated hopping parameter, and spin-orbit coupling strength. The results show that the absorption response is strongly affected by the superconducting gap and by changes in the quasiparticle spectrum. Rashba spin-orbit coupling changes the density of states and therefore modifies the absorption. These results show that hopping processes, interaction strength, and spin-orbit coupling play important roles in the ultrasonic and electromagnetic absorption of superconducting systems.

</details>


### [67] [When Repulsion Creates Pairing: A New Perspective on Unconventional Superconductivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03022)
*Patrick Navez*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The paper proposes a Kohn-Luttinger-like formalism for cuprates where superexchange dynamics of virtual holes in oxygen orbitals provides the screening necessary for effective electron attraction, yielding a semi-quantitative temperature-doping phase diagram consistent with observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the mechanism of electron/hole attraction in cuprates despite repulsive Coulomb forces, building on the Kohn-Luttinger concept of dynamical screening leading to Cooper pair formation.

Method: A formalism adapted from Kohn-Luttinger theory and BCS theory, where screening arises from superexchange dynamics of virtual holes in the oxygen orbitals of the CuO₂ plane.

Result: Semi-quantitative predictions for the temperature-doping phase diagram including pseudo-gap, strange metal, antiferromagnetism, superconducting, and normal states, in agreement with observations.

Conclusion: The superexchange-mediated screening mechanism can produce effective attraction between carriers in cuprates, and the resulting BCS-inspired model captures key features of the cuprate phase diagram.

Abstract: Despite the repulsive Coulomb law and the Pauli statistics that do not favor bound states, attraction between electrons or holes is nevertheless possible in the context of many body interaction and of valley potential landscapes, reminiscent of exotic superconducting materials. In particular, in 1965, Kohn and Luttinger published a note revealing that the dynamical screening of the repulsive Coulomb interaction leads, under certain conditions, to an effective attraction necessary for the formation of Cooper pairs. We propose such a formalism adapted to the cuprates, where the screening arises from the superexchange dynamics of virtual holes in the oxygen orbitals of the $Cu O_2$ plane. Inspired from the Bardeen-Copper-Schrieffer (BCS) theory, we can derive some predictions for the temperature-doping phase diagram (pseudo-gap, strange metal, antiferromagnetism, superconducting, and normal states) in semi-quantitative agreement with observations.

</details>


### [68] [Josephson effects in the spin-triplet superconductor/altermagnet/spin-triplet superconductor junctions: the detection of the intrinsic $\bf{d}$-vector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03685)
*Ya-Ting Han,Li-Juan Chen,Wen-Ting Liu,Qiang Cheng,Qing-Feng Sun*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This paper theoretically investigates Josephson effects in spin-triplet superconductor/altermagnet/spin-triplet superconductor junctions using Green's function method, finding that current-phase relationships depend on d-vector direction and altermagnet orientation, with 0-π transitions achievable by rotating the d-vector, offering a magnetic-field-free scheme for detecting the intrinsic d-vector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To detect the intrinsic direction of the d-vector in spin-triplet superconductors while avoiding the negative influence of magnetic fields on the d-vector, which is a limitation in existing detection approaches.

Method: Green's function method applied to spin-triplet superconductor/altermagnet/spin-triplet superconductor Josephson junctions, systematically varying the d-vector direction, altermagnet orientation angle, and altermagnetic strength.

Result: (1) Current-phase relationships strongly depend on d-vector direction and altermagnet orientation. (2) 0-π transitions occur when the d-vector is rotated at a given orientation angle. (3) Critical current varies systematically with d-vector direction, orientation angle, and altermagnetism strength.

Conclusion: Altermagnetic Josephson junctions can provide distinguishable information about the d-vector direction without magnetic field interference, offering a feasible detection scheme for the intrinsic d-vector, and the 0-π transition has potential applications in quantum device design.

Abstract: We study the Josephson effects in the spin-triplet superconductor/altermagnet/spin-triplet superconductor junctions using the Green's function method. It is found that the current-phase difference relationships in the junctions strongly depend on the direction of the $\bf{d}$-vectors in the spin-triplet superconductors and the orientation angle of the altermagnet. For the given orientation angle, the $0$-$π$ transition can be obtained when the $\bf{d}$-vector is rotated. The variations of the critical current of the junctions with the direction of the $\bf{d}$-vector, the orientation angle and the strength of altermagnetism are systematically investigated. These Josephson effects can provide the distinguishable information about the direction of the $\bf{d}$-vector. Compared to the existing research, the proposed altermagnetic Josephson junctions can effectively avoid the negative influence of the magnetic field on the $\bf{d}$-vector and can serve as a feasible scheme for the detection of the intrinsic $\bf{d}$-vector. The obtained $0$-$π$ transition in the junctions can also have potential applications in the design of quantum devices.

</details>


### [69] [Tuning Superconductivity by Isovalent Antimony Substitution in PrFeAs(O,F)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03712)
*Priya Singh,Konrad Kwatek,Tatiana Zajarniuk,Taras Palasyuk,Cezariusz Jastrzębski,Michał Wierzbicki,Tomasz Cetner,Andrzej Szewczyk,Shiv J. Singh*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This study investigates Sb substitution at the As site in fluorine-doped PrFeAsO iron pnictides, finding that moderate Sb doping (x≤0.3) slightly suppresses Tc from 48K to 44K, while higher doping rapidly destroys superconductivity due to disorder and secondary phases. Moderate doping enhances upper critical fields and vortex pinning but poor grain connectivity limits critical current density. The system exhibits a crossover from electronically tuned to disorder-dominated superconductivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how isovalent substitution at the pnictogen site affects the structural, electronic, and superconducting properties of iron pnictide superconductors, and to explore the crossover between electronically tuned and disorder-dominated regimes.

Method: Structural analysis, Raman spectroscopy, density functional theory (DFT), transport, magnetotransport (up to 9 T), and magnetic measurements on PrFeAs1-xSbxO0.7F0.3 (x=0 to 1.0).

Result: Tc decreases gradually from ~48K to ~44K for x≤0.3, then rapidly suppressed at higher x. Raman/DFT show lattice expansion and softening of pnictogen modes upon Sb substitution. Moderate Sb doping enhances upper critical fields and vortex activation energy (stronger pinning), but critical current density remains low due to poor intergranular connectivity.

Conclusion: A crossover from electronically tuned superconducting state to disorder-dominated regime occurs in isovalently substituted iron pnictides.

Abstract: We investigate the effects of isovalent Sb substitution at the As site in fluorine-doped PrFeAs1-xSbxO0.7F0.3 (x = 0 to 1.0) through structural, Raman spectroscopy, density functional theory (DFT), transport, magnetotransport, and magnetic measurements. The superconducting transition temperature decreases gradually from ~48 K for the parent compound to ~44 K up to x = 0.3, followed by a rapid suppression at higher Sb concentrations due to increasing disorder and secondary phase formation. Raman spectroscopy and DFT reveal lattice expansion and pronounced softening of pnictogen related vibrational modes upon Sb substitution. Magnetotransport measurements up to 9 T show enhanced upper critical fields and increased vortex activation energy for moderate Sb doping, indicating stronger vortex pinning. However, the critical current density remains low because of poor intergranular connectivity. The results demonstrate a crossover from an electronically tuned superconducting state to a disorder-dominated regime in isovalently substituted iron pnictides.

</details>


### [70] [Evidence of the Cooper-Pair Field with Gaussian Memory Kernel in Unconventional Superconductors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03937)
*Udomsilp Pinsook*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The paper develops a dynamical theory of the superconducting pair field where the Hubbard-Stratonovich field Δ is treated as a memory-dressed Bogoliubov pair field with a Gaussian memory kernel. In cuprates, the antinodal gap acts as a momentum-space cavity, selecting fluctuation wavevectors that can become charge-density-wave instabilities. The framework yields resonant algebraic prefactors forming a hierarchy (p = -1/2, 1/2, 1, 3/2, ...) with a Gaussian cutoff. Two channels emerge: a pseudogap memory channel and a condensate-assisted coherent channel below Tc. Comparisons with Raman, ARPES, and tunneling data support a Gaussian-memory pair continuum interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a dynamical description of the superconducting pair field beyond the static order parameter paradigm, treating it as a memory-dressed Bogoliubov field coupled to collective fluctuations, particularly for understanding cuprate superconductors.

Method: Starting from the standard pair-field effective action, the Cooper-channel Hubbard-Stratonovich field Δ is coupled to antinode-selected collective or self-generated fields. Ensemble averaging over a Gaussian distribution of local Bogoliubov frequencies yields a memory factor exp[-t²/(2τ_g²)]. The antinodal gap acts as a momentum-space spectral cavity selecting fluctuation wavevectors. The response is expressed using parabolic-cylinder functions.

Result: The framework produces resonant algebraic prefactors forming a hierarchy p = -1/2, 1/2, 1, 3/2, ... with a Gaussian envelope cutoff. Two spectral channels emerge: an incoherent pseudogap memory channel and a condensate-assisted coherent channel proportional to |Δ₀(T)|² below Tc. Numerical results compare favorably with experimental data on Bi₂Sr₂CaCu₂O₈₊δ.

Conclusion: The superconducting transition primarily reorganizes pair-field spectral weight between incoherent pseudogap memory and coherent Bogoliubov memory channels. Raman, ARPES, tunneling, and doping-dependent ARPES scaling are complementary projections of the same Gaussian-memory pair continuum.

Abstract: We develop a dynamical description of the superconducting pair field in which the Cooper-channel Hubbard--Stratonovich field $Δ$ is treated as a memory-dressed Bogoliubov pair field rather than as a purely static order parameter. Starting from the standard pair-field effective action, we couple $Δ$ to antinode-selected collective or self-generated fields. An ensemble of such modes produces a distribution of local Bogoliubov frequencies; when this distribution is approximately Gaussian, ensemble averaging gives the memory factor $\exp[-t^2/(2τ_g^2)]$. In cuprate superconductors, the antinodal gap or pseudogap restricts the active electronic phase space and acts as a momentum-space spectral cavity. It selects fluctuation wavevectors $\mathbf Q_a$ that may become charge-density-wave-like instabilities in an ordered limit, but behave as a reservoir of local collective fields in the fluctuating regime. The same framework admits resonant algebraic prefactors, so that threshold and forced-oscillator responses generate the hierarchy $p=-1/2,1/2,1,3/2,\ldots$, while the Gaussian envelope cuts off secular growth and converts these branches into finite spectral components. The resulting picture contains a robust pseudogap memory channel and, below $T_c$, an additional condensate-assisted coherent channel proportional to $|Δ_0(T)|^2$. Thus the superconducting transition primarily reorganizes pair-field spectral weight between incoherent pseudogap memory and coherent Bogoliubov memory. The frequency-domain response is expressed in terms of parabolic-cylinder functions, and comparisons with Raman, ARPES, tunneling, and doping-dependent ARPES scaling suggest that these probes are complementary projections of the same Gaussian-memory pair continuum. We compare our numerical results with the recent experimental data on Bi$_2$Sr$_2$CaCu$_2$O$_{8+δ}$.

</details>


### [71] [Full Gate-Voltage Control of a Parity-Protected Superconducting Qubit with an Altermagnetic Josephson Junction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04097)
*Guo-Liang Guo*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The paper proposes a parity-protected superconducting qubit using a gate-tunable superconductor-altermagnet-superconductor Josephson junction, where interference between two spin sectors creates a 0-π transition with suppressed first harmonic, enabling a double-well potential with opposite-parity degenerate states. All-electrical control and estimated coherence times up to tens of milliseconds suggest a scalable route for protected qubits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Parity-protected superconducting qubits offer long coherence but typically require magnetic-flux biasing, introducing flux noise, control overhead, and limited scalability. The authors aim to eliminate these issues by using altermagnets, which provide spin-dependent functionality without external magnetic fields.

Method: The qubit is built from a superconductor-altermagnet-superconductor Josephson junction. Altermagnets (compensated magnets with zero net magnetization but momentum-dependent spin splitting) enable two spin sectors to acquire opposite phase shifts, creating two interfering Josephson channels. By electrically tuning the chemical potential to the 0-π transition, the first Josephson harmonic is suppressed, and the second harmonic dominates, forming a double-well potential with two nearly degenerate opposite-parity Cooper-pair states.

Result: For realistic gatemon-compatible parameters, the estimated coherence times reach up to tens of milliseconds. The qubit operations are fully gate-controlled, avoiding magnetic flux biasing.

Conclusion: Altermagnetic Josephson junctions provide a promising platform for protected superconducting qubits with all-electrical, local, and scalable control, overcoming key limitations of existing parity-protected qubit designs.

Abstract: Parity-protected superconducting qubits offer intrinsically long coherence, but many current implementations require magnetic-flux biasing, which introduces flux noise, control overhead, and limited scalability. Here we propose a parity-protected qubit based on a gate-tunable superconductor-altermagnet-superconductor Josephson junction. Altermagnets are compensated magnets with momentum-dependent spin splitting and zero net magnetization, providing spin-dependent functionality without external magnetic fields. In the proposed junction, the two spin sectors acquire opposite phase shifts, generating two Josephson channels whose interference is controlled electrically by the chemical potential. At the tuned $0$-$π$ transition, the first Josephson harmonic is strongly suppressed while the second harmonic dominates, yielding a double-well potential with two nearly degenerate states of opposite Cooper-pair parity. For realistic gatemon-compatible parameters, we estimate coherence times of up to tens of milliseconds while maintaining fully gate-controlled qubit operations. These results establish altermagnetic Josephson junctions as a promising route toward protected superconducting qubits with local, scalable, and all-electrical control.

</details>


### [72] [Vortex-Number-Controlled Josephson Diode Polarity in Corbino Junctions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04104)
*Linghao Huang,Jing Wang*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The Josephson diode effect polarity in Corbino junctions can be controlled by vortex number via spatial harmonic filtering of inhomogeneities, producing polarity reversal without requiring Majorana physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To demonstrate that vortex-number control of Josephson diode polarity is a generic consequence of structured spatial inhomogeneity, and to show that vortex-parity-dependent polarity reversal is not an exclusive signature of Majorana physics.

Method: Solving the continuum Andreev spectral problem; effective one-dimensional edge model and full two-dimensional lattice model numerical simulations.

Result: Vortex number selectively filters specific spatial Fourier harmonics of local inhomogeneity, reshaping amplitudes and relative phases of higher-order Josephson current harmonics, reversing critical-current asymmetry.

Conclusion: Vortex-controlled diode polarity reversal arises generically from geometric and structural inhomogeneities in Corbino junctions, not exclusively from Majorana physics.

Abstract: We demonstrate that the polarity of the Josephson diode effect in Corbino Josephson junctions can be deterministically controlled by the vortex number, which arises as a generic consequence of structured spatial inhomogeneity. By solving the continuum Andreev spectral problem, we identify a mechanism in which the vortex number selectively filters specific spatial Fourier harmonics of the local inhomogeneity. This harmonic selection reshapes the amplitudes and relative phases of higher-order Josephson current harmonics, ultimately reversing the critical-current asymmetry. Numerical simulations of both an effective one-dimensional edge model and full two-dimensional lattice models confirm the robustness of this mechanism. Crucially, our results show that a vortex-parity-dependent reversal of diode polarity is not an exclusive signature of Majorana physics, but can emerge generically from geometric and structural inhomogeneities in Corbino junctions.

</details>


### [73] [Hall Coefficient Sign Reversal Driven by Orbital-Selective Oxygen-Vacancy Scattering in Nickelate Films](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04122)
*Jian-Jian Miao,Yue Liu,Yue Zhao,Yichen Hua,Changming Yue,Wei-Qiang Chen*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: Hall sign reversals in bilayer nickelate films arise from orbital-selective oxygen-vacancy scattering, not band filling alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hall measurements in superconducting bilayer nickelate films exhibit sign reversals that rigid-band electron doping cannot explain, motivating a deeper microscopic transport mechanism.

Method: DFT+CDMFT-derived correlated multi-orbital quasiparticle model combined with T-matrix treatment of oxygen-vacancy scattering within a semiclassical Boltzmann transport framework.

Result: In-plane vacancies selectively suppress the d_{x^2-y^2} orbital transport channel and drive R_H through zero; inner-apical vacancies make R_H more negative. Multiband compensation alone is insufficient.

Conclusion: Pocket-resolved, orbital-selective oxygen-vacancy scattering is the microscopic origin of Hall sign reversal, establishing a framework for oxygen-stoichiometry-dependent transport in nickelate films.

Abstract: Hall measurements in superconducting bilayer nickelate films show sign reversals that cannot be explained by rigid-band electron doping alone. We combine a DFT+CDMFT-derived correlated multi-orbital quasiparticle model with a $T$-matrix treatment of oxygen-vacancy scattering in a semiclassical Boltzmann transport framework. We find that multiband compensation is insufficient by itself: in-plane vacancies selectively suppress the transport channel dominated by the $d_{x^2-y^2}$ orbital and drive $R_H$ through zero, whereas inner-apical vacancies make $R_H$ more negative. These results identify pocket-resolved and orbital-selective oxygen-vacancy scattering as the microscopic origin of the Hall coefficient sign reversal and provide a framework for oxygen-stoichiometry-dependent transport in nickelate films.

</details>


### [74] [Pseudo-superconducting-diode effect in ferroelectric Josephson junctions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04398)
*Yaozu Tang,Nienke ten Haaf,Artem Bondarenko,Mazhar N. Ali,Yaroslav M. Blanter*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The paper proposes a magnetic-field-free pseudo-superconducting-diode effect in ferroelectric Josephson junctions, where polarization switching induces asymmetric critical and retrapping currents, enabling supercurrent rectification without external magnetic fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve supercurrent rectification (SDE) without requiring an external magnetic field, enabling simpler and more compact nonreciprocal superconducting devices.

Method: A coupled dynamical model combining a polarization-dependent RCSJ (Resistively and Capacitively Shunted Junction) description with Landau-Khalatnikov-Tani ferroelectric dynamics.

Result: Ferroelectric polarization switching induces asymmetric critical and retrapping currents under current sweeps; nonreciprocity is highly tunable via ferroelectric parameters and sweep protocol and remains robust at finite temperatures.

Conclusion: Ferroelectric Josephson junctions are a promising platform for magnetic-field-free nonreciprocal superconducting devices.

Abstract: The superconducting diode effect (SDE), characterized by unequal critical supercurrents in opposite current directions, enables supercurrent rectification. We propose a magnetic-field-free pseudo-superconducting-diode effect in ferroelectric Josephson junctions with broken inversion symmetry. Using a coupled dynamical model that combines a polarization-dependent RCSJ description with Landau-Khalatnikov-Tani ferroelectric dynamics, we show that ferroelectric polarization switching induces asymmetric critical and retrapping currents under current sweeps. The resulting nonreciprocity is highly tunable via ferroelectric parameters and the sweep protocol and remains robust at finite temperatures. Our work identifies ferroelectric Josephson junctions as a promising platform for magnetic-field-free nonreciprocal superconducting devices.

</details>


### [75] [Superconductivity on the verge of metal-insulator transition in Cu$_{1-x}$Zn$_x$Ir$_2$S$_4$ probed by $μ$SR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04628)
*K. M. Kojima,M. Miyazaki,M. Hiraishi,A. Koda,R. Kadono,C. Baines,Y. Tsuchiya,H. S. Suzuki,H. Kitazawa*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The paper investigates superconductivity in Zn-substituted CuIr₂S₄ (Cu₁₋ₓZnₓIr₂S₄) using μSR, finding s-wave pairing in the dirty limit (ℓ ≪ ξ₀), suggesting that strong electron scattering smears out unconventional pairing anisotropy, and links this to high-pressure superconductivity in CuIr₂S₄.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the nature of superconductivity induced by Zn substitution in CuIr₂S₄, which suppresses the metal-insulator transition, and to explore potential connections to high-pressure superconductivity in the parent compound.

Method: Muon spin rotation and relaxation (μSR) measurements to probe the temperature and field dependence of superfluid density; resistivity measurements to determine normal-state transport properties.

Result: The superfluid density is consistent with fully gapped s-wave pairing; normal-state resistivity is high (10⁻³–10⁻² Ω·cm), placing the system in the dirty limit (ℓ ≪ ξ₀).

Conclusion: The Zn-induced superconductivity in CuIr₂S₄ is conventional s-wave in the dirty limit, where strong electron scattering eliminates any potential pairing anisotropy, and shares similarities with the high-pressure superconductivity in the parent compound.

Abstract: The thiospinel CuIr$_2$S$_4$ undergoes a metal-insulator transition below $\approx$230 K, which is suppressed by substitution of Cu with Zn (Cu$_{1-x}$Zn$_x$Ir$_2$S$_4$) to induce superconductivity for $0.2\lesssim x\lesssim0.8$. We show that the temperature/field dependence of superfluid density in samples with $x = 0.3$ and 0.4 ($T_{\rm c} \approx 3$ K and 2.5 K) investigated by muon spin rotation and relaxation ($μ$SR) is consistent with a fully gapped $s$-wave pairing. Meanwhile, the relatively high resistivity (10$^{-3}$-10$^{-2}$ $Ω\:$cm) in their normal state suggests that the superconductivity is in the "dirty limit" where the mean free path is much shorter than the coherence length ($\ell \ll ξ_0$). This indicates that the potential anisotropy associated with unconventional pairing mechanisms expected under the strong electron correlations is smeared out by the electron scattering. Based on these observations, we discuss potential link between the Zn substitution-induced superconductivity and that recently discovered in CuIr$_2$S$_4$ under high pressure ($>18$ GPa) where the existence of strong electron scattering is also suggested.

</details>


### [76] [$H-T$ Phase diagram of CeRh$_{2}$As$_{2}$: Refinement of the parity-switch scenario](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04928)
*Changhee Lee,P. M. R. Brydon*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: CeRh₂As₂ exhibits anomalous superconducting phase behavior under magnetic fields, which can be explained by nonsymmorphic band structure and coexisting antiferromagnetic order, rather than the simple parity-switch scenario.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies between experimental observations (vertical high-field phase boundary, anomalous initial slope of high-field SC phase) and the parity-switch scenario for CeRh₂As₂'s multiple superconducting phases and first-order transitions in magnetic fields.

Method: Combined analysis of symmetry-enforced nonsymmorphic band structure (focusing on Dirac node and type-II Van Hove saddle points near the X point in the Brillouin zone) and a phenomenological theory incorporating coexisting antiferromagnetic order.

Result: (1) Anisotropic band structure due to increased interlayer hopping explains the anomalous initial slope of the odd-parity SC phase. (2) Field-enhanced odd-parity superconductivity from over-suppression of Pauli depairing in the presence of antiferromagnetism explains the nearly vertical thermodynamic phase boundary.

Conclusion: The unconventional superconducting phase diagrams of CeRh₂As₂ arise from the interplay between nonsymmorphic band structure anisotropy and coexisting antiferromagnetic order, resolving discrepancies with the simpler parity-switch scenario.

Abstract: The superconductivity of CeRh$_2$As$_2$ has drawn attention due to its first-order transition in magnetic fields. At first glance, the multiple superconducting (SC) phases as well as the first-order transition appear consistent with the parity-switch scenario, which emphasizes the role of strong Rashba spin-orbit coupling enabled by the locally non-centrosymmetric crystal structure. However, experimental phase diagrams exhibit notable deviations from this simple picture: thermodynamic measurements reveal a nearly vertical phase boundary of the high-field SC phase despite the orbital depairing effect, while transport measurements show that the initial slope of the high-field SC phase is steeper than that of the low-field SC phase. Here, we show that these discrepancies can be understood by considering the combined effects of nonsymmorphic band structure and coexisting antiferromagnetic order. We demonstrate that the symmetry-enforced electronic structure around the Dirac node and type-II Van Hove saddle points near the X point in the Brillouin zone boundary becomes more anisotropic with increased interlayer hopping amplitude, and this anisotropic band structure naturally accounts for the anomalous initial slope of the odd-parity SC phase. Meanwhile, a phenomenological theory incorporating coexisting antiferromagnetism explains the nearly vertical thermodynamic phase boundary as a consequence of field-enhanced odd-parity superconductivity enabled by the over-suppression of Pauli depairing.

</details>


### [77] [Electronic theory for scanning tunneling microscopy spectra in bilayer nickelate thin films](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31569)
*Marius Scholten,Steffen Bötzel,Frank Lechermann,Peayush Choubey,Ilya M. Eremin*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The paper theoretically analyzes STM spectra in bilayer nickelate superconductors using a two-orbital model, showing that the multiorbital character and spatial dependence of Wannier functions enable band-resolved identification of superconducting gap features and gamma-band incipiency through tip-position-dependent measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent STM experiments on superconducting bilayer nickelate La2PrNi2O7 films have measured gap features, but the band-selective identification and Cooper-pairing models remain debated. The authors aim to provide a theoretical framework for interpreting these STM spectra to resolve ambiguities.

Method: The authors employ a realistic two-orbital bilayer model combined with the continuum Green's function formalism to theoretically compute orbital- and band-selective local density of states and STM spectra. They analyze the spatial dependence of Wannier functions and impurity scattering effects.

Result: The multiorbital character and spatial dependence of Wannier functions cause STM spectra to develop characteristic features that depend on the tip position. This enables band-resolved analysis of superconducting coherence peaks and scattering momenta.

Conclusion: The work identifies a clear experimental pathway to identify the debated incipiency of the gamma-band and determine the band origins of coherence peaks through distance-dependent LDOS measurements and impurity scattering corrections.

Abstract: Recent Scanning Tunneling Microscopy (STM) experiments measuring the superconducting gap features in thin films of superconducting bilayer nickelates La2PrNi2O7 at ambient pressure and compressive strain paved the way to study the Cooper-pairing models and the band-selective identification of the gap features in these systems. Here, using the realistic two-orbital bilayer model and the continuum Green's function formalism, we theoretically analyze orbital and band-selective local density of states as well as the corresponding STM spectra. We find that the multiorbital character and the spatial dependence of the Wannier functions leads to the spectra developing characteristic features depending on the position of the scanning tunneling microscope's tip. This allows for a band-resolved analysis of the superconducting coherence peaks and scattering momenta. We identify a clear path for experimental measurements to not only identify the debated incipiency of the gamma-band, but also identification of the coherence peaks' band origins via distance dependent measurements of the local density of states and its corrections through impurity scattering.

</details>
