The incorporation of nitrogen enhances visible-light absorption, promotes efficient charge separation, and improves structural robustness, making these materials highly suitable for photocatalytic applications. In this study, (MnFeCoNiCu)N-based HEN nanoparticles (NPs) were synthesized as visible-light-assisted photocatalysts for the degradation of antibiotics, including sulfamethoxazole (SME) and tetracycline (TCL). The catalyst exhibited excellent performance, achieving 96% degradation of SME and 94% removal of TCL within 2 h of visible-light irradiation. The photocatalytic activity was systematically evaluated under varying operational parameters, including solution pH, catalyst dosage, pollutant concentration, and the presence of coexisting ions. Notably, the catalyst maintained high efficiency in real water matrices, demonstrating its practical applicability. The entropy-stabilized nitride framework exhibited negligible metal leaching, excellent thermal and structural stability as confirmed by temperature-dependent synchrotron angle-dispersive X-ray diffraction, and stable performance over multiple reuse cycles. Furthermore, in situ liquid-cell transmission electron microscopy provided real-time insight into catalyst-pollutant interactions, while complementary molecular simulations revealed the structural stability of the HEN NPs during molecular adsorption and the distinct interaction modes of the two antibiotics. Phytotoxicity tests using Vigna radiata confirmed the effective detoxification of treated solutions. Overall, this work establishes (MnFeCoNiCu)N HENs as efficient and durable visible-light-driven photocatalysts for the removal of antibiotics from complex aqueous environments.

</details>


### [106] [Magnetic ordering of a van der Waals material combining vastly different magnetic anisotropies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02759)
*K. K. Pokhrel,S. Ray,N. Machavcova,A. Koliogiorgos,K. Carva*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: VI3 exhibits two coexisting V configurations with strikingly different magnetic anisotropy; atomistic spin-dynamics simulations show that their ratio controls the Curie temperature and explains the unusual thickness dependence (monolayer TC > bulk TC), with experimental agreement at ~1:1 ratio.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the unusual thickness dependence of VI3 (monolayer TC higher than bulk) and the role of coexisting vanadium configurations with distinct magnetic anisotropy.

Method: Atomistic spin-dynamics simulations informed by first-principles calculations, analyzing single-ion anisotropy and exchange interactions.

Result: Strong anisotropy contrast raises ordering temperature; V-atom inhomogeneity weakens interlayer order while preserving intralayer correlations; experimental TC values reproduced at ~1:1 ratio; monolayer TC reproduced with slightly modified ratio.

Conclusion: The coexistence of two V configurations explains VI3's unique magnetic behavior, and TC can be tuned by controlling their relative occupation.

Abstract: Among magnetic van der Waals materials, the vanadium trihalide family exhibits unique features. In particular, VI3 contains V atoms of two different types, as two energetically close electronic occupations can coexist in real samples. These types show strikingly different magnetic anisotropy, predicted to differ by more than an order of magnitude. The combination forms a distinctive magnetic system. VI3 also displays an unusual thickness dependence: the monolayer Curie temperature (TC) is higher than that of the bulk, contrary to the expectation that interlayer coupling reinforces magnetic order. Using atomistic spin-dynamics simulations informed by first-principles calculations, we investigate the critical temperature behavior from the combined perspective of single-ion anisotropy and exchange interactions. The strong anisotropy contrast significantly affects thermal stability: increasing the fraction of high-anisotropy sites raises the energy cost of transverse spin fluctuations and increases the ordering temperature. Furthermore, V-atom inhomogeneity makes the interlayer super-superexchange network spatially nonuniform, creating competing exchange pathways. This weakens coherent interlayer order while preserving robust intralayer ferromagnetic correlations, thus modifying the bulk-monolayer TC relation. Our model reproduces experimental TC values when the ratio of the two V types is close to 1:1, in agreement with two experimental methods. This supports the coexistence of two V configurations in VI3. The monolayer TC is reproduced with a slightly modified ratio, possibly linked to polaron concentration. The sensitivity of TC to this ratio suggests that the ordering temperature of VI3 can in principle be tuned over a broad range by controlling the relative occupation of the two vanadium configurations.

</details>


### [107] [First principles study of chalcogen vacancy effect on the optoelectronic and photocatalytic properties of transition metal dichalcogenides monolayers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02776)
*Tahir Wahab,Antonio Cammarata,Tomas Polcar*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: This paper uses quantum mechanical calculations to study how chalcogen vacancies in 2D TMDC monolayers (MX₂, M=Mo,W; X=S,Se) affect their optoelectronic and photocatalytic properties. Vacancies reduce band gaps, create electrostatic asymmetry aiding charge separation, enable hydrogen evolution reaction, and make WS₂/WSe₂ suitable for CO₂ reduction, suggesting defective TMDCs have valuable applications rather than being discarded as flaws.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Chalcogen vacancies spontaneously occur in 2D TMDC monolayers under working conditions, and their impact on optoelectronic and photocatalytic properties needs to be systematically understood to determine whether such defects are detrimental or potentially beneficial.

Method: Quantum mechanical calculations (DFT-based) on prototypical MX₂ (M=Mo,W; X=S,Se) monolayers, including structural optimization, band structure calculations, and work function/electrostatic potential analysis for both pristine and vacancy-containing structures.

Result: M-X bond lengths around vacancies differ from pristine structures; vacancies introduce electronic states near the Fermi level, reducing the band gap; vacancies induce electrostatic potential asymmetry facilitating charge separation; all defective structures can perform hydrogen evolution reaction (HER), but need co-catalyst for oxygen evolution reaction (OER); WS₂ and WSe₂ defective monolayers can reduce CO₂ into useful products.

Conclusion: Vacancy-containing TMDC monolayers possess enhanced photocatalytic capabilities compared to pristine counterparts, should not be regarded as flawed products, and the results may guide experimental synthesis of vacancy-engineered MX₂ monolayers for optoelectronic and photocatalytic applications.

Abstract: In working conditions, chalcogen vacancies spontaneously occur in two-dimensional transition metal dichalcogenides (TMDCs) monolayers, affecting their optoelectronic and photocatalytic properties. To study how chalcogen vacancies affect such properties, we use quantum mechanical calculations considering prototypical MX$_2$ (M = Mo, W, X = S and Se) TMDCs monolayers. Structural optimisations show that M-X bond lengths about a vacancy are different compared to the bond lengths in the pristine structure. Band structure calculations reveal that the introduction of vacancies produce electronic states about the Fermi level, hence resulting in the reduction of the band gap. Work function and electrostatic potential calculations show that the introduction of vacancies induce an asymmetry in the electrostatic potential facilitating the charge separation; such feature is absent in a pristine monolayer. All the considered defective structures are capable of performing hydrogen evolution reaction, while co-catalyst is required to perform oxygen evolution reaction when used for water splitting. WS$_2$ and WSe$_2$ defective monolayers can serve as an efficient photocatalytic material for reducing CO$_2$ into useful chemical products. The presented results show that vacancy-containing TMDCs monolayers own photocatalytic capabilities compared to the pristine counterparts, thus showing that defective TMD monolayers have prospective applications and should not be regarded as flawed products to be discarded. Finally, the results might constitute guidelines for the experimental synthesis of vacancy-engineered MX$_2$ monolayers for optoelectronic devices and photocatalytic applications.

</details>


### [108] [Twist-configured moire-moire reconstruction governs diverse commensurate double-moire phases in twisted bilayer graphene on h-BN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02822)
*Yuta Seo,Naoto Nakatsuji,Jimpei Kawase,Naoto Hishida,Kenji Watanabe,Takashi Taniguchi,Takuto Kawakami,Mikito Koshino,Tomoki Machida*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: The paper investigates how multiple coexisting moire lattices in twisted bilayer graphene on h-BN reconstruct and interact, revealing moire-moire reconstruction as a general framework for engineering structural and electronic order across length scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how distinct coexisting moire patterns in van der Waals heterostructures interact and reconstruct, addressing the fundamental question of moire lattice interaction beyond isolated single-moire systems.

Method: Conductive atomic force microscopy combined with continuum-model simulations to study twisted bilayer graphene on hexagonal boron nitride.

Result: Moire-moire reconstruction manifests across multiple length scales, producing diverse commensurate double-moire phases. The stacking registry between moire lattices is determined by global twist configuration (helical vs. alternate) via rotational relaxation, and together with twist angle and strain, governs commensurate domains ranging from C3z-symmetric to symmetry-modified structures.

Conclusion: Moire-moire reconstruction provides a general framework for engineering structural and electronic order—including theoretically predicted topological flat bands below the magic angle—in multilayer moire materials.

Abstract: The coexistence of multiple moire lattices in van der Waals heterostructures raises a fundamental question: how do distinct moire patterns interact and reconstruct? Here, we investigate twisted bilayer graphene (tBG) on hexagonal boron nitride (h-BN), where tBG and graphene/h-BN moire structures coexist, using conductive atomic force microscopy combined with continuum-model simulations. We show that reconstruction between these moire lattices-moire-moire reconstruction-manifests across multiple length scales, giving rise to diverse commensurate double-moire phases. Locally, the stacking registry between the two moire lattices is uniquely selected by the global twist configuration (helical or alternate), mediated by rotational relaxation of the shared graphene layer. This registry, together with twist angle and strain, governs commensurate domains from C3z-symmetric to strained symmetry-modified structures. These results establish moire-moire reconstruction as a general framework for engineering structural and electronic order -- including theoretically predicted topological flat bands below the magic angle -- in multilayer moire materials.

</details>


### [109] [Crystal structure and basic properties of dirhenate quantum materials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02848)
*Danrui Ni,Xianghan Xu,Stephen Zhang,N. P. Ong,Sanfeng Wu,R. J. Cava*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: The paper synthesizes and characterizes M(ReO4)2 (M = Mn-Zn, Mg) quantum materials with triangular-lattice layered structures, revealing low-temperature long-range magnetic order that is easily suppressed by small magnetic fields, indicating competing interactions in a low-dimensional framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To synthesize and characterize the previously underexplored anhydrous divalent 3d-metal dirhenate quantum materials and investigate their magnetic behavior, particularly the presence of competing magnetic interactions in a low-dimensional triangular lattice.

Method: Solid-state synthesis of M(ReO4)2 for M = Mn through Zn and Mg; crystal structure refinement via single crystal X-ray diffraction; characterization of optical absorption, magnetism, and heat capacity.

Result: Many M(ReO4)2 phases exhibit long-range magnetic order at low temperatures, and these ordered states are highly sensitive to applied magnetic fields, being suppressed by relatively small fields.

Conclusion: The observed field-sensitive magnetic ordering and ease of suppression suggest competing magnetic interactions in a low-dimensional (triangular lattice) framework, warranting further investigation of these quantum materials.

Abstract: The anhydrous divalent 3d-metal dirhenate quantum materials, M(ReO$_4$)$_2$,were synthesized using solid-state methods for M = Mn through Zn. Previously unreported Mg(ReO4)2 is also described. Their layered crystal structures, which feature an in-plane triangular lattice of M$^{2+}$, were refined using single crystal X-ray diffraction, and their optical absorption and several other physical properties were characterized. Their magnetism and heat capacity reveal long-range magnetic order at low temperatures in many of the M(ReO$_4$)$_2$ phases. Notably, many of these ordered states are sensitive to applied magnetic fields and can be readily suppressed by relatively small fields, suggesting competing magnetic interactions in a low-dimensional framework, which appear worthy of further study.

</details>


### [110] [Thermophysical and mechanical properties of UFe$_2$ fabricated by spark plasma sintering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02918)
*Yifan Sun,Hironobu Nakamura,Daisuke Okada,Hiroaki Muta,Yuji Ohishi,Ken Kurosaki*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: This study fabricated and characterized dense polycrystalline UFe₂, measuring its thermal conductivity (10→25 W/m·K from 306 to 1073 K) and mechanical properties (E=69 GPa, G=24 GPa, Hᵥ=5.6 GPa), revealing it is more thermally conductive and mechanically compliant than Fe₂Zr and Fe₂B, which has implications for Fukushima fuel debris management.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Following the Fukushima Daiichi accident, safe retrieval and management of fuel debris requires reliable thermophysical and mechanical property data. The U-Fe system is critical due to abundant iron from in-vessel stainless steel structures, but high-temperature thermophysical properties of UFe₂ were lacking, with prior studies focusing mainly on magnetic and electronic properties.

Method: Dense, nearly single-phase polycrystalline UFe₂ was fabricated via arc melting followed by spark plasma sintering. Thermal and mechanical properties were characterized from room temperature to 1073 K.

Result: Thermal conductivity increased monotonically from 10 W/m·K at 306 K to 25 W/m·K at 1073 K, exceeding those of Fe₂Zr and Fe₂B at high temperatures. UFe₂ is mechanically more compliant, with Young's modulus E=69 GPa, shear modulus G=24 GPa, and Vickers hardness Hᵥ=5.6 GPa, all below those of both Fe intermetallics.

Conclusion: During decommissioning, thermal-management and structural evaluations should account for the comparatively high-conductivity and mechanically compliant nature of UFe₂ within the heterogeneous fuel debris.

Abstract: Following the accident at the Fukushima Daiichi Nuclear Power Plant in 2011, core meltdown produced fuel debris whose safe retrieval and management require reliable thermophysical and mechanical property data. Among the metallic phases identified in the debris, the U-Fe system is particularly important because of the abundant iron originating from in-vessel stainless steel structures. However, within this system, the high-temperature thermophysical properties of UFe$_2$ have received relatively little attention, with most prior studies focusing on its magnetic and electronic properties. To fill this data gap in the literature, we fabricated dense, nearly single-phase polycrystalline UFe$_2$ by arc melting followed by spark plasma sintering, and characterized its thermal and mechanical properties from room temperature to 1073 K. Results show that the thermal conductivity of UFe$_2$ increased monotonically from 10 Wm$^{-1}$K$^{-1}$ at 306 K to 25 Wm$^{-1}$K$^{-1}$ at 1073 K, surpassing those of the iron intermetallics Fe$_2$Zr and Fe$_2$B at high temperatures. In addition, UFe$_2$ is mechanically more compliant, displaying a Young's modulus $E$ of 69 GPa, a shear modulus $G$ of 24 GPa, and a Vickers hardness $H_{\mathrm{V}}$ of 5.6 GPa, all well below those of both Fe intermetallics. Consequently, during decommissioning, thermal-management and structural evaluations should take into account the comparatively high-conductivity and mechanically compliant nature of UFe$_2$ within the heterogeneous fuel debris.

</details>


### [111] [Chiropiezoelectric Energy Harvesting from Lattice-Handedness-Controlled Selenium Nanowires](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03063)
*Oleksii Ohiienko,Wookjin Jung,Jihyeon Yeom*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: The paper introduces atomic chirality engineering as a new design principle for enhancing piezoelectricity in inorganic nanomaterials, demonstrated using chiral selenium nanowires where crystal handedness alone (left vs. right) produces different piezoelectric performance, enabling improved energy harvesting and sensing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing strategies (composition optimization, defect engineering, strain engineering, orientation control) have not introduced a new mechanism for enhancing piezoelectricity, while the demand for wearable electronics and soft robotics requires improved inorganic piezoelectric materials for harvesting weak biomechanical energy.

Method: The authors propose atomic chirality engineering, altering crystal lattice handedness to control dipole alignment and electromechanical coupling. They use atomically chiral trigonal selenium nanowires as a model system and characterize performance via piezoresponse force microscopy, as well as fabricating flexible nanogenerators and self-powered acoustic sensors from both enantiomers.

Result: Right-handed D-Se nanowires achieve a higher effective piezoelectric coefficient than left-handed counterparts. Nanogenerators and acoustic sensors built from the two enantiomers produce distinct outputs under identical deformation, confirming that crystal handedness alone changes piezoelectric performance at identical chemical composition.

Conclusion: Atomic chirality is established as a distinct route for designing piezoelectric materials, potentially extendable across non-centrosymmetric inorganic semiconductors for sustainable energy harvesting and wearable sensing.

Abstract: The growth of wearable electronics, soft robotics, and Internet-of-Things systems has intensified the demand for inorganic piezoelectric materials that harvest weak biomechanical energy. Advances through composition optimization, defect engineering, strain engineering, and orientation control have improved existing electromechanical responses, but have not introduced a new mechanism for enhancing piezoelectricity. Here we propose atomic chirality engineering as a design principle for inorganic piezoelectric nanomaterials. Unlike conventional strategies that modify composition or morphology, atomic chirality alters the handedness of the crystal lattice itself, adding a degree of freedom for controlling dipole alignment, electromechanical coupling, and potentially spin-dependent transport. Using atomically chiral trigonal selenium nanowires as a model system, we show that crystal handedness alone changes piezoelectric performance at identical chemical composition. Piezoresponse force microscopy resolved a consistent enantiomeric difference, with right-handed D-Se nanowires reaching a higher effective piezoelectric coefficient than their left-handed counterparts, and flexible nanogenerators and self-powered acoustic sensors built from the two enantiomers produced distinct outputs under identical deformation. This work establishes atomic chirality as a distinct route for designing piezoelectric materials, one that may extend across non-centrosymmetric inorganic semiconductors for sustainable energy harvesting and wearable sensing.

</details>


### [112] [Self-Driven Atomic Dispersion in Graphitic Layers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03137)
*Zhaoxi Chen,Yulu He,Zhuoran Yao,Jian Liu,Jun Cai,Ziyi Fan,Wenjun Zhang,Lei Lei,Zupeng Chen,Bo Yang,Zhi Liu,Zhu-Jun Wang*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: Metal nanoparticles can self-disperse into single atoms under hydrocarbon oxidation conditions via carbon growth, CO-induced weakening, and defective carbon trapping, forming high-performance single-atom catalysts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how metal nanoparticles transform into isolated single atoms within carbon supports, a key but unclear mechanism for maximizing metal utilization in single-atom catalysts.

Method: Using Pt-catalyzed hydrocarbon oxidation as a model, the study combines operando electron microscopy, near-ambient-pressure X-ray photoelectron spectroscopy, and mass spectrometry to track structural and chemical evolution in real time.

Result: Graphitic carbon grows at Pt step edges, reconstructing surfaces and generating undercoordinated sites for atom release; in-situ CO accumulates at the metal-carbon interface, weakening bonding and promoting self-amplified atom migration; defective carbon overlayers trap and transport liberated atoms; oxidative etching preserves interfacial gas access. Similar behavior observed across other metals.

Conclusion: This self-driven dispersion pathway represents a general mechanism for single-atom catalyst synthesis, yielding products with electrocatalytic hydrogen production activity exceeding standard commercial benchmarks.

Abstract: Carbon-supported single-atom catalysts maximize metal utilization, but how metal nanoparticles transform into isolated atoms within carbon remains unclear. We show that metal nanoparticles can undergo a self-driven dispersion process under hydrocarbon oxidation conditions, transforming into single atoms that are confined in carbon matrix. Using Pt-catalysed hydrocarbon oxidation as a model, we combine operando electron microscopy, near-ambient-pressure X-ray photoelectron spectroscopy and mass spectrometry to track coupled structural and chemical evolution. Graphitic carbon grows at step edges of Pt nanoparticle, continuously reconstructing Pt surface and generating undercoordinated sites for atom release. In-situ generated CO accumulates at the metal-carbon interface, weakening bonding and facilitating self-amplified atom release and migration. Defective carbon overlayers then trap, stabilize and transport liberated atoms, while oxidative etching preserves interfacial access of reaction-gas. Similar behaviour across other metals suggests a general atomization pathway for single-atom catalyst synthesis, yielding products with electrocatalytic hydrogen production activity beyond standard commercial benchmarks.

</details>


### [113] [Martensitic Transformation in Crystal-Amorphous Superlattices of NiTi Shape Memory Alloy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03172)
*Bhavna Singh,Shivam Tripathi*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: Large-scale molecular dynamics simulations reveal that crystal-amorphous superlattices in NiTi shape memory alloys fundamentally alter martensitic transformation pathways, introducing second-order-like precursor transformations, reducing thermal hysteresis from 275 K to 95-110 K, and increasing elastic modulus and critical stress by 60-90%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Tailoring SMA properties through coherent second phases is effective but limited by difficulty identifying thermodynamically compatible phases; crystal-amorphous superlattices (CAS), where the second phase derives from the base material, offer an alternative, but the influence of partial amorphization on martensitic transformation remains unexplored.

Method: Large-scale molecular dynamics simulations investigating temperature- and stress-induced martensitic transformations in CAS-NiTi with varying crystalline phase fractions.

Result: Partial amorphization introduces an initial continuous (second-order-like) transformation before the first-order martensitic transformation, reduces thermal hysteresis from 275 K to 95-110 K, and increases elastic modulus and critical stress by 60-90% depending on crystalline fraction.

Conclusion: Crystal-amorphous superlattices are a promising microstructural design strategy for tailoring the thermo-mechanical performance of shape memory alloys.

Abstract: Shape memory alloys (SMAs) exhibit unique thermo-mechanical properties arising from reversible martensitic transformation. Tailoring these properties through coherent second phases is effective but is often limited by the difficulty of identifying thermodynamically compatible phases. Crystal-amorphous superlattices (CAS), in which the second phase is derived from the base material itself, provide an attractive alternative. However, the influence of partial amorphization on martensitic transformation and thermo-mechanical behavior remains largely unexplored. Here, large-scale molecular dynamics simulations are used to investigate temperature- and stress-induced martensitic transformations in CAS-NiTi with different crystalline phase fractions. Partial amorphization fundamentally modifies the transformation pathway, introducing an initial continuous (second-order-like) transformation before the conventional first-order martensitic transformation. The amorphous phase also enhances transformation reversibility, reducing the thermal hysteresis from 275 K in fully crystalline NiTi to 95-110 K in CAS-NiTi. Simultaneously, the elastic modulus and the critical stress for stress-induced martensitic transformation increase by approximately 60-90 percent, depending on the crystalline fraction. These improvements originate from heterogeneous nucleation at crystal-amorphous interfaces, retained austenite that promotes the reverse transformation, and mechanical constraint imposed by the amorphous phase. These findings establish crystal-amorphous superlattices as a promising microstructural design strategy for tailoring the thermo-mechanical performance of shape memory alloys.

</details>


### [114] [Data-driven multi-objective optimization for alloy recycling using factorization machines and quantum annealing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03208)
*Thomas Plehn,Katrin Bugelnig,Silvana Tumminello,Daniel Barragan-Yani,David Melching*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: This paper assesses a factorization-machine and quantum-annealing workflow (FM+QA) for multi-objective Pareto optimization in metal up-cycling through alloy design, using data-driven Tchebycheff scalarization. Results show FM+QA extends QUBO-based optimization to materials discovery with multiple competing objectives, with analysis of scaling behavior and comparisons to classical simulated annealing under different encoding strategies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether quantum annealing can provide practical quantum advantage for complex optimization tasks, specifically for multi-objective Pareto optimization in technologically relevant materials design (metal up-cycling through alloy design).

Method: Integrated factorization-machine and quantum-annealing workflow (FM+QA) with data-driven Tchebycheff scalarization (DDTS) to handle the non-convex Pareto front; compared against classical simulated annealing using both regular binary encoding and one-hot encoding.

Result: FM+QA extends QUBO-based optimization to data-driven materials discovery with multiple competing objectives; scaling behavior analyzed; comparison with classical SA under different encoding strategies provided.

Conclusion: The paper provides a critical perspective on problem sizes and encoding strategies where quantum-annealing-based optimization may become practically beneficial in the near future.

Abstract: Quantum annealing has the potential to provide practical quantum advantage for complex optimization tasks. Here, we present a systematic assessment of an integrated factorization-machine and quantum-annealing workflow (FM+QA) for a technologically relevant application: multi-objective Pareto optimization in metal up-cycling through alloy design. To address the non-convex nature of the Pareto front, we employ the recently proposed data-driven Tchebycheff scalarization (DDTS) scheme. Our results show that FM+QA extends the applicability of QUBO-based optimization to data-driven materials discovery problems with multiple competing objectives. In particular, we analyze the scaling behavior of the approach and compare quantum annealing with classical simulated annealing using both regular binary encoding and one-hot encoding. Finally, we provide a critical perspective on the problem sizes and encoding strategies for which quantum-annealing-based optimization may become practically beneficial in the near future.

</details>


### [115] [Enhanced hydrogen response of copper-doped TiO$_2$ synthesised by helium-assisted magnetron sputtering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03268)
*Akash Kumar,Nirmal Kumar,David Kolenatý,Mina Farahani,Jiří Rezek,Tomáš Kozák,Stanislav Haviar*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: Using He in sputtering gas to grow Cu-doped TiO₂ thin films enhances nanostructuring, porosity, and anatase content, boosting H₂ sensor response from 1.4 to 6.0 at 300°C for NAD films, with GLAD films showing modest improvement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether replacing Ar with He in reactive DC magnetron sputtering can tailor microstructure and enhance hydrogen sensing performance of Cu-doped TiO₂ thin films.

Method: Cu-doped TiO₂ thin films were deposited by reactive DC magnetron sputtering in Ar/O₂/He mixtures with varying He fraction, using both normal-angle deposition (NAD) and glancing-angle deposition (GLAD), followed by post-deposition annealing. Structural characterization was done via XRD and electron microscopy; sensing performance was tested at 300°C in 1 vol.% H₂. A simulation-supported growth model was used to interpret nanostructuring.

Result: He promotes nanostructuring, lattice expansion in as-deposited NAD films, increased porosity after annealing, and stronger anatase phase. NAD film response increased from 1.4 to 6.0 with He addition; GLAD films showed only modest improvement.

Conclusion: Helium-assisted sputtering is a useful physical route for tailoring oxide thin film morphology and gas-sensing properties, with the nanostructuring attributed to energetic backscattered He, reduced hammering effect, and suppression of adatom mobility.

Abstract: Cu-doped TiO$_2$ thin films for hydrogen sensing were synthesised by reactive DC magnetron sputtering in Ar/O$_2$/He mixtures, with the He fraction used as a control parameter for film growth. By combining normal-angle deposition (NAD) and glancing-angle deposition (GLAD) with post-deposition annealing, the effects of He on microstructure formation and sensor performance were examined. X-ray diffraction and electron microscopy revealed that He promotes nanostructuring, lattice expansion in as-deposited NAD films, increased porosity after annealing, and a stronger anatase character in the final oxide layers. These structural changes, which enhance the reactive surface area, lead to improved hydrogen sensing at 300\,$^\circ$C in 1~vol.\,\% H$_2$. The response of NAD films increased from 1.4 to 6.0 simply by replacing part of the argon with helium, whereas GLAD films showed only a modest increase. The observed nanostructuring is discussed in terms of a simulation-supported growth scenario involving energetic backscattered He, a reduced hammering effect, and cooling-related suppression of adatom mobility, which together favour the formation of a more open sensing layer. Helium-assisted sputtering represents a useful physical route for tailoring oxide thin films for gas-sensing applications.

</details>


### [116] [NanoBTE: Fast Iterative Solution of the Phonon Boltzmann Transport Equation for Nanoscale Heat Transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03226)
*Hongjiang Chen,Hai-Xuan Lin,Xiaole Tian,Hongyu Chen,Juan Zhang,Shengyao Yan,Hezhu Shao,Yu Wu,Junjie Liu,Hao Zhang*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: NanoBTE is a deterministic finite-volume solver for the non-gray phonon Boltzmann transport equation, supporting complex geometries, band-resolved phonon properties, multiple boundary conditions, and efficient MPI/GPU parallelization for nanoscale heat dissipation simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Nanoscale heat dissipation is a critical challenge in advanced semiconductor devices, where classical Fourier's law fails due to boundary scattering and ballistic phonon transport.

Method: A deterministic finite-volume solver for the non-gray phonon BTE under relaxation-time approximation, supporting 2D/3D geometries, discrete-ordinates angular quadrature, multiple boundary conditions, sequential/synthetic iterative schemes, and band-direction task decomposition for MPI/GPU parallelization.

Result: Efficient MPI-based CPU parallelization and GPU acceleration of sparse transport operations, with synthetic iterative scheme accelerating convergence in near-diffusive regimes.

Conclusion: NanoBTE provides an efficient, parallelized framework for multiscale phonon transport simulation beyond Fourier's law in complex nanoscale semiconductor geometries.

Abstract: Nanoscale heat dissipation has become a critical challenge in advanced semiconductor devices, where phonon transport can strongly deviate from the classical Fourier description owing to boundary scattering and ballistic effects. Here, we propose NanoBTE, a deterministic finite-volume solver for the non-gray phonon Boltzmann transport equation under the relaxation-time approximation. NanoBTE supports complex two- and three-dimensional geometries, band-resolved phonon properties, discrete-ordinates angular quadrature, volumetric heat generation, and multiple phonon boundary conditions, including thermalizing, diffuse, and specular reflections. To improve the efficiency of multiscale simulations, we implement both sequential and synthetic iterative schemes, with the latter coupling the microscopic phonon transport equation to a macroscopic diffusion-type temperature equation to accelerate convergence in near-diffusive regimes. Furthermore, NanoBTE adopts a band-direction task decomposition strategy, enabling efficient MPI-based CPU parallelization and GPU acceleration of the dominant sparse transport operations.

</details>


### [117] [Anomalous Structural Response of Quasi-One-Dimensional Antiferromagnetic Metal KMn6Bi5 under high pressure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03287)
*Hanming Ma,Qingxin Dong,Tong Shi,Xiaoli Ma,Zhongjin Wu,Shaoheng Ruan,Pengtao Yang,Zhaoming Tian,Jianping Sun,Yoshiya Uwatoko,Genfu Chen,Xiaohui Yu,Bosen Wang,Jinguang Cheng*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: High-pressure X-ray diffraction on KMn6Bi5 up to 12.5 GPa reveals anisotropic compressibility, a structural anomaly near 11 GPa, and anomalous hardening of Mn nanotubes (5–11 GPa) followed by configuration optimization, correlating with the pressure-temperature phase diagram and chemical pressure effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how pressure modulates the atomic coordination environment in the Q1D antiferromagnetic metal KMn6Bi5 and how such modulation governs the stability of electronic orders in low-dimensional systems.

Method: High-pressure single-crystal X-ray diffraction measurements up to 12.5 GPa, with detailed structural analysis of lattice parameters, anisotropic compressibility, and Mn nanotube behavior.

Result: Pronounced anisotropic compressibility (a/a0=0.91, b/b0=0.94 at 12.5 GPa); a structural anomaly near 11 GPa without symmetry breaking; anomalous hardening of Mn nanotubes between 5–11 GPa; configuration optimization of Mn/Bi nanotubes around 11 GPa.

Conclusion: The observed structural features correlate with the pressure-temperature phase diagram and mimic chemical pressure effects from alkali metal substitution, providing microscopic insight into how coordination environment modulation stabilizes electronic orders in low-dimensional systems.

Abstract: We report high-pressure single-crystal X-ray diffraction measurements on the quasi-one-dimensional (Q1D) antiferromagnetic metal KMn6Bi5 up to 12.5 GPa, revealing the detailed pressure evolution of its atomic coordination environment. We find that the lattice exhibits pronounced anisotropic compressibility-the relative changes in the a and b lattice parameters reach a/a0=0.91 and b/b0 = 0.94 at 12.5 GPa-and a distinct structural anomaly emerges near 11 GPa without any symmetry-breaking. Detailed structural analysis further uncovers an anomalous hardening of the Mn nanotubes between 5 and 11 GPa, followed by a configuration optimization of the Mn/Bi nanotubes around 11 GPa. These features correlate closely with the reported pressure-temperature phase diagram of KMn6Bi5 and compare favorably with the chemical pressure effects induced by substituting K with Na, Rb, or Cs. Our findings provide key microscopic insights into how coordination environment modulation governs the stability of electronic orders in low-dimensional systems.

</details>


### [118] [Phase-field modeling of elastically driven abnormal grain growth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05298)
*Yazhuo Liu,Yin Zhang,Kunqing Ding,Yichen Yang,Alejandro Barrios,Xavier Maeder,Olivier Pierron,Xing Liu,Ting Zhu*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: This paper investigates abnormal grain growth (AGG) in ultrafine-grained Ni thin films under cyclic loading at room temperature within the elastic deformation regime. Using phase-field simulations and micromechanical analysis, they show that elastic energy reduction drives orientation-selective grain growth, with local grain geometry and stress states controlling the growth pathway. The framework is generalized to polycrystalline materials with varying elastic anisotropy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Grain-refined metals suffer from grain coarsening under thermo-mechanical loading, limiting their engineering applications. Recent experiments observed abnormal grain growth in ultrafine-grained Ni thin films under cyclic loading at room temperature within macroscopic elastic deformation—a regime where such growth was not expected—motivating the need to understand the underlying physical mechanisms.

Method: Phase-field simulations combined with micromechanical analysis.

Result: Elastic energy reduction provides a thermodynamically plausible driving force for orientation-selective grain growth. Local grain geometry and stress states critically determine the grain growth pathway. The framework is applicable to systems with varying elastic anisotropy.

Conclusion: Elastically driven abnormal grain growth is a general phenomenon in polycrystalline materials; the established phase-field/micromechanical framework can be used to investigate and predict such behavior across different material systems with elastic anisotropy.

Abstract: Grain-refined metals typically exhibit high strength, yet their engineering applications are often constrained by grain coarsening under thermo-mechanical loading. Recent experiments have revealed abnormal grain growth (AGG) in ultrafine-grained Ni thin films subjected to cyclic loading at room temperature. Unlike conventional AGG, which generally requires significant plastic deformation or high temperatures, this phenomenon occurs within the regime of macroscopic elastic deformation. This AGG is characterized by the preferential growth of grains with an in-plane <100> orientation aligned with the loading direction. Here, we investigate the underlying physical mechanisms by combining phase-field simulations with micromechanical analysis. The results indicate that elastic energy reduction provides a thermodynamically plausible driving force for this orientation-selective grain growth. Phase-field simulations reveal the evolution kinetics of AGG and confirm that local grain geometry and stress states play critical roles in determining the grain growth pathway. By applying this framework to systems with varying elastic anisotropy, we establish a general approach for investigating elastically driven AGG in polycrystalline materials.

</details>


### [119] [Data-driven atomistic modelling of hybrid halide perovskite passivation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05321)
*Laura-Bianca Paşca,Henry J. Snaith,Volker L. Deringer*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: The paper presents a multistep training pipeline for machine-learned interatomic potentials, using a large general perovskite dataset (hyP-26) and fine-tuning with a specialized dataset, to model molecular passivation of halide perovskite surfaces, specifically amino-silane molecules on mixed-composition surfaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Molecular passivation of surface defects is key to improving optoelectronic performance of hybrid halide perovskites, but the atomistic mechanisms remain incompletely understood. Existing machine-learned interatomic potentials are difficult to apply to experimentally-realistic scenarios like molecules coordinating to perovskite surfaces.

Method: A multistep training pipeline resembling continuous fine-tuning used for large language models, consisting of: (i) a large curated open dataset (hyP-26) of diverse metal and hybrid halide perovskite structures; and (ii) a small specialized dataset for an amino-silane molecule passivating the surface for fine-tuning.

Result: The approach successfully models collective behavior at a mixed-composition halide perovskite surface passivated with varying coverage of amino-silane molecules, revealing an evolution of interactions with increasing molecular surface coverage.

Conclusion: The multistep fine-tuning pipeline enables accurate atomistic modelling of experimentally-realistic passivation scenarios, analogous to continuous fine-tuning strategies in large language models.

Abstract: Molecular passivation of surface defects is key to improving the optoelectronic performance of hybrid halide perovskite materials, but the underlying atomistic mechanisms are incompletely understood. While machine-learned interatomic potentials are now widely used to simulate complex molecular and crystalline systems, their application to experimentally-realistic scenarios - such as molecules coordinating to perovskite surfaces - is still far from trivial. Here, we describe a multistep training pipeline, resembling continuous fine-tuning used for large language models, to underpin atomistic modelling and computational experiments in this domain. Our protocol involves two components: (i) a large, curated, and open dataset of diverse metal and hybrid halide perovskite structures ('hyP-26'); and (ii) a small, specialised dataset for an amino-silane molecule passivating the surface, providing highly specific information for fine-tuning. We apply this approach to explore collective behaviour at a mixed-composition halide perovskite surface passivated with a varying coverage of amino-silane molecules, revealing an evolution of interactions with increasing molecular surface coverage.

</details>


### [120] [Topological Electronic States and Phonon Mediated Superconductivity in Ru Based Ternary Pnictides: ZrRuAs and HfRuP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04651)
*Jubair Hossan Abir,Tanvir Khan,Tauhidur Rahman,Md. Kamrul Hassan,Sraboni Saha Moly,Mst. Maskura Khatun,Raihana Shams Islam,Saleh Hasan Naqib*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: This paper investigates the structural, electronic, mechanical, optical, thermodynamic, and superconducting properties of ZrRuAs and HfRuP, finding them to be topological semimetals with ductile mechanical behavior, metallic reflectivity, UV absorption, and conventional superconductivity with transition temperatures of 9.75 K and 9.03 K respectively.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To fill existing research gaps in understanding the physical and superconducting properties of the topological superconducting materials ZrRuAs and HfRuP.

Method: First-principles electronic band structure calculations including spin-orbit coupling, analysis of Fermi surface topology, mechanical property evaluation via Pugh ratio, Poisson ratio, and machinability index, optical property calculations, thermodynamic stability analysis, and estimation of superconducting transition temperatures.

Result: Both ZrRuAs and HfRuP exhibit topological semimetallic behavior with bands crossing the Fermi level; spin-orbit coupling lifts band degeneracies; Fermi surfaces contain both electron and hole pockets; both compounds are ductile with excellent machinability; they show metallic reflectivity and strong UV absorption; superconducting Tc is 9.75 K for ZrRuAs and 9.03 K for HfRuP, indicating strong-coupling conventional superconductivity.

Conclusion: A detailed understanding of the physical and superconducting properties of ZrRuAs and HfRuP has been achieved, closing existing research gaps in these topological superconducting materials.

Abstract: This study investigates the structural, electronic, mechanical, optical, thermodynamic, and superconducting properties of ZrRuAs and HfRuP. Electronic band structure calculations reveal topological semimetallic behavior in both compounds with several bands crossing the Fermi level. The inclusion of spin orbit coupling lifts band degeneracies in both compounds. The Fermi surface shows both electron and hole segments. The estimated Pugh ratio, Poisson ratio and machinability index indicate that both compounds are ductile in nature and exhibit excellent machinability. Optical results show metallic reflectivity and notable absorption in the ultraviolet region. Thermodynamic analysis indicates stable behavior. The estimated superconducting transition temperatures are 9.75 K for ZrRuAs and 9.03 K for HfRuP that indicates strong coupling conventional superconductivity. The results provide a detailed view of the physical and superconducting properties of ZrRuAs and HfRuP and help to close the existing research gaps in these topological superconducting materials.

</details>


### [121] [Color Centers in Cubic Boron Nitride](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03322)
*William Stenlund,Joel Davidsson,Viktor Ivády,Rickard Armiento,Igor A. Abrikosov*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: A large-scale high-throughput DFT screening of >8000 point defects in c-BN was performed to identify color centers suitable for quantum technologies, with hybrid functional calculations pinpointing ONVB (explaining GC-2 line), two carbon defects, and NaB- as promising candidates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cubic boron nitride (c-BN) is a wide-bandgap semiconductor with potential in power electronics and quantum technologies. Color centers in WBGS can serve as single photon emitters and quantum sensors, but several experimentally measured zero phonon lines in c-BN remain unidentified, motivating a systematic computational search.

Method: A large-scale point defect dataset for c-BN was generated using density functional theory (DFT) within the high-throughput Automatic Defect Analysis and Qualification (ADAQ) workflow. More than 8000 defects (s- and p-elements) were calculated in various charge and spin states. The results were stored in a defect database, filtered for NV-diamond-like properties, and promising candidates were further refined with hybrid functional calculations.

Result: The ONVB defect was reexamined and likely explains the GC-2 line. Hybrid calculations also identified other defect candidates with bright emission, including two carbon defects and the NaB- defect.

Conclusion: The high-throughput computational screening approach successfully identified several promising color center candidates in c-BN, with the ONVB defect assigned to the GC-2 line, demonstrating the power of systematic defect discovery in combinatorial chemical spaces for quantum technology applications.

Abstract: Cubic boron nitride (c-BN) is a wide-bandgap semiconductor (WBGS) with potential applications in both power electronics and quantum technologies. Color centers in WBGS can be used as single photon emitters and quantum sensors. Several zero phonon lines have been measured in c-BN experiment but not yet identified. To systematically probe the combinatorially complex chemical space of defects, we generate a large-scale point defect data set for c-BN. We apply density functional theory calculation implemented in a high-throughput workflow Automatic Defect Analysis and Qualification (ADAQ) to broadly screen for point defect complexes containing s- or p-elements. More than 8000 defects have been calculated in different charge and spin states. The calculated properties are stored in defect database and are then filtered to find defects with properties similar to the NV-center in diamond. More accurate calculations using hybrid functionals are then performed on a selected set of promising defects to further assess their suitability for quantum technology. In particular, we reexamined the ONVB defect which likely explains the GC-2 line. The hybrid calculations also suggest other defect candidates with bright emission, such as two carbon defects and the NaB- defect.

</details>


### [122] [Computational Determination of Optimal Growth Protocols for Metastable Polymorphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03371)
*Simon B. Hollweger,Anna Werkovits,Tadeas Lesovsky,Oliver T. Hofmann*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: This paper develops an optimal control theory framework combining kinetic Monte Carlo simulations with a nucleation-and-growth model to design time-dependent temperature and pressure protocols that maximize the yield of metastable surface polymorphs at organic-inorganic interfaces, achieving 97% yield compared to 73% from a manually optimized three-step protocol.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reliably growing desired target structures at organic-inorganic interfaces remains challenging, especially when the target structure is thermodynamically metastable. Identifying growth conditions that selectively produce a given surface structure is difficult, but specific interface structures can exhibit superior properties.

Method: Combined kinetic Monte Carlo simulations with a parameterized nucleation-and-growth model and applied optimal control theory to predict growth recipes that maximize yield of the desired metastable target structure.

Result: The optimized control trajectory increased the yield of the desired metastable phase from 73% (manually optimized three-step protocol) to 97% for the same total protocol duration.

Conclusion: Time-dependent temperature and pressure protocols can be optimized via optimal control theory to promote high-yield formation of metastable surface polymorphs, guiding the system through phase space while avoiding kinetic growth regimes that favor the thermodynamically stable structure.

Abstract: The reliable growth of a desired target structure remains a central challenge for organic-inorganic interfaces. Specific interface structures can exhibit properties that are superior compared to those of other possible interface structures, but identifying growth conditions that selectively produce a given surface structure is difficult, particularly when the target structure is thermodynamically metastable. Here, we demonstrate how time-dependent temperature and pressure protocols can be optimized to promote the high-yield formation of a metastable surface polymorph. To this end, we combine kinetic Monte Carlo simulations with a parameterized nucleation-and-growth model and apply optimal control theory to predict growth recipes that maximize the yield of the desired target structure. Applying this approach to a prototypical model of an organic molecules adsorbed on a metal surface, we identify experimentally plausible protocols that guide the system through phase space while avoiding kinetic growth regimes in which formation of the thermodynamically stable structure is favored. Compared to a manually optimized three-step protocol, the optimized control trajectory increases the yield of the desired metastable phase from 73 % to 97 % for the same total protocol duration.

</details>


### [123] [Dyna-Mat: End-to-end benchmarking of foundation machine learning interatomic potentials in finite-temperature ensembles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03433)
*Mikołaj J. Gawkowski,Nongnuch Artrith,Silvia Bonfanti,Abhijeet Sadashiv Gangan,Hendrik H. Heenen,Joseph Kioseoglou,Ivor Lončarić,Hemanadhan Myneni,Janosh Riebesell,Mariana Rossi,Matthias Rupp,Jonathan Schmidt,Shubham Sharma,Benjamin X. Shi,Antoni Wadowski,Lukas Hörmann,Venkat Kapil*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: Dyna-Mat-v1.0 is a benchmark dataset of first-principles molecular dynamics trajectories for evaluating foundation MLIPs at finite temperatures. Testing 15 models across four tiers reveals that lower force errors generally correlate with better observables, but pressure prediction remains poor, and some systems show qualitative failures despite low force errors. Cross-trained models approach Pareto-optimal accuracy-cost trade-offs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Foundation MLIPs are increasingly used as drop-in replacements for first-principles calculations, but their accuracy on structural and dynamical observables in finite thermodynamic ensembles remains unestablished due to lack of ground truth data.

Method: The authors introduce Dyna-Mat-v1.0, a benchmark dataset of condensed-phase first-principles molecular dynamics trajectories, and evaluate 15 foundation MLIPs across four model tiers by comparing single-point energy/force errors on first-principles configurations and observables from MLIP-driven trajectories. An accuracy-cost Pareto frontier is constructed.

Result: Models with lower single-point force errors "on average" yield lower errors for structural/dynamical observables, but individual systems show qualitative structural failures despite low force errors. Pressure is poorly described across most models. Latest cross-trained models are near Pareto-optimal.

Conclusion: End-to-end finite-temperature validation is essential for quantifying predictive behavior of foundation MLIPs. Dyna-Mat-v1.0 provides a simple, scalable route for assessing them beyond static and harmonic benchmarks relevant to materials design.

Abstract: Foundation machine learning interatomic potentials (MLIPs) are increasingly being used as drop-in replacements for first-principles calculations, enabling simulations of materials at length and time scales that were previously inaccessible. However, due to lack of ground truth data, their accuracy on structural and dynamical observables in finite thermodynamic ensembles is yet to be established. Here, we introduce Dyna-Mat-v1.0, a benchmark dataset of condensed-phase first-principles molecular dynamics trajectories designed to test foundation MLIPs at realistic finite-temperature conditions. Using this dataset, we evaluate 15 foundation MLIPs across four model tiers by comparing both single-point energy and force errors on first-principles configurations and observables generated from MLIP-driven trajectories. We find that "on average" models with lower single-point force errors also yield lower errors for structural and dynamical observables. However, there are individual systems for which low force errors lead to qualitative failures in the predicted structure. Pressure remains poorly described across most models, pointing to limitations in the density functional theory stress labels available in current large-scale training datasets. Finally, we construct an accuracy-cost Pareto frontier to identify the best trade-offs for molecular dynamics with foundation MLIPs, finding that the latest generation of cross-trained models is close to Pareto-optimal according to the accuracy metrics considered here. Overall, Dyna-Mat-v1.0 shows that end-to-end finite-temperature validation is essential for quantifying the predictive behaviour of foundation MLIPs, and provides a simple, scalable route for assessing them beyond static and harmonic benchmarks relevant to materials design.

</details>


### [124] [From Stacking Disorder to Cubic Order: Ice Crystallization from Deeply Supercooled Water](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03465)
*Yulin Lin,Weimin Guo,Suvo Banik,Tao Zhou,Thomas E. Gage,Lei Yu,Maksim A. Sultanov,Martin Holt,Subramanian Sankaranarayanan,Peng Zhang,Ilke Arslan,Jianguo Wen*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: Using in situ cryo-TEM with a membrane-encapsulated microdroplet platform, this study visualizes freezing of deeply supercooled water at molecular resolution. Stacking-disordered ice with mixed hexagonal/cubic sequences forms, where cubic ice monolayers gradually thicken, nucleating defect-free cubic germs on basal planes that act as facet-registered kinetic seeds, enforcing cubic twinning and sequential branching to produce eight-branched dendrites with global octahedral symmetry despite local disorder. Later, latent heat triggers a hexagonal phase transition while preserving the pre-established cubic symmetry, revealing how strong kinetic driving forces convert microscopic disorder into emergent macroscopic symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To elucidate the microscopic mechanisms by which atomic-scale disorder leads to emergent macroscopic order during crystallization far from equilibrium, specifically in deeply supercooled water.

Method: In situ cryogenic transmission electron microscopy (cryo-TEM) with a membrane-encapsulated microdroplet platform for molecular-resolution visualization of freezing dynamics.

Result: Stacking-disordered ice with mixed hexagonal/cubic sequences forms initially, with cubic ice existing as isolated monolayers. Gradual thickening of cubic layers leads to nucleation of defect-free cubic germs on basal planes, which act as facet-registered seeds enforcing cubic twinning and sequential branch multiplication, producing robust eight-branched dendrites with global octahedral symmetry despite local stacking disorder. Latent heat later drives a hexagonal phase transition, preserving the pre-established cubic symmetry.

Conclusion: Strong kinetic driving forces can convert microscopic disorder into emergent macroscopic symmetry, providing a general framework for understanding and controlling rapid crystallization far from equilibrium.

Abstract: Crystallization far from equilibrium can generate morphologies that defy classical crystal habits, yet the microscopic mechanisms linking atomic-scale disorder to emergent macroscopic order remain elusive. Here we use in situ cryogenic transmission electron microscopy with a membrane-encapsulated microdroplet platform to directly visualize the freezing of deeply supercooled water at molecular resolution. We show that homogeneous nucleation produces stacking-disordered ice composed of mixed hexagonal and cubic sequences, in which cubic ice initially exists only as isolated monolayers. The gradual thickening of these cubic layers constitutes the key kinetic mechanism that governs the entire crystallization pathway. As thickening proceeds, nanoscale, defect-free cubic ice germs nucleate on the basal planes of the disordered lattice. These faceted cubic germs act as facet-registered kinetic seeds that enforce cubic twinning and sequentially multiply growth branches. This kinetic pathway reproducibly generates robust eight-branched dendrites with global cubic (octahedral) symmetry, even though each branch remains highly stacking-disordered. At later stages, latent heat release drives a crossover to the thermodynamically favored hexagonal phase; remarkably, the pre-established global cubic symmetry is retained. These results reveal how strong kinetic driving forces convert microscopic disorder into emergent macroscopic symmetry, providing a general framework for understanding and controlling rapid crystallization far from equilibrium.

</details>


### [125] [Physics adapted generative AI for metal insulator transition materials under label scarcity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03578)
*Gourab Datta,Sarah Sharif,Zhisheng Shi,Yaser Banad*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: This paper argues that generative AI for discovering metal-insulator-transition (MIT) materials should focus on mechanism-informed phase-transition hypotheses rather than just searching for stable crystal structures. It proposes a modular physics-adapted framework with staged verification of phase competition, electronic contrast, transition-pathway plausibility, and control accessibility to guide credible discovery under label scarcity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Experimentally verified metal-insulator-transition (MIT) materials remain limited and their underlying mechanisms are often complex. The authors argue that current generative AI approaches for MIT discovery, which focus only on stable crystal structures, are insufficient.

Method: The authors propose a modular physics-adapted framework that moves beyond stable crystal structure search and instead prioritizes mechanism-informed phase-transition hypotheses. The framework incorporates staged verification of four key aspects: phase competition, electronic contrast, transition-pathway plausibility, and control accessibility.

Result: The proposed framework is designed to guide credible MIT material discovery under severe label scarcity conditions, addressing the limitations of existing generative AI approaches.

Conclusion: Generative AI for MIT discovery should shift focus from finding stable crystal structures to mechanism-informed phase-transition hypotheses, using a modular physics-adapted framework with staged verification to enable credible discovery despite limited experimental data.

Abstract: Metal-insulator-transition (MIT) materials are promising candidates for switchable electronics, neuromorphic hardware, and reconfigurable photonics, yet experimentally verified examples remain limited and the underlying mechanisms are often complex. We argue that generative AI for MIT discovery should move beyond the search for stable crystal structures alone and instead prioritize mechanism-informed phase-transition hypotheses. A modular physics-adapted framework, combined with staged verification of phase competition, electronic contrast, transition-pathway plausibility, and control accessibility, can guide credible discovery under severe label scarcity.

</details>


### [126] [Beyond the Parasitic Limit: A Nanoprobing Framework for De-embedding Intrinsic Ferroelectric Properties at the Deep Sub-Micrometer Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03588)
*Niklas Kyoushi,Georg Schönweger,Ole Gronenberg,Dirk Meyners,Adrian Petraru,Fabian Lofink,Simon Fichtner*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: The paper introduces a quantitative in-situ nanoprobing framework to resolve true electrical responses of ferroelectric capacitors down to 165 nm, overcoming parasitic and geometric artifacts using a 'Screened Power Law' model, conductance scaling, and leakage-compensation protocols on 20 nm AlScN.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The continued scaling of ferroelectric devices for next-generation computing is challenged by a metrological bottleneck: at deep sub-micrometer scales, intrinsic material properties are masked by extrinsic parasitic impedances and geometric fringing fields.

Method: The framework uses in-situ nanoprobing without lithographic bond pads; a non-linear 'Screened Power Law' model decouples attofarad-level device capacitances from near-field probe interactions; conductance scaling analysis overcomes geometric dilution artifacts; leakage-compensation and noise filtering extract intrinsic hysteresis loops.

Result: Successfully resolved true electrical response of ferroelectric capacitors down to 165 nm diameter in AlScN, decoupled parasitic effects, and extracted pristine intrinsic C-V and J-E hysteresis loops in the discrete few-grain limit.

Conclusion: The framework provides a universal analytical toolkit to overcome measurement limits of deep-submicron ferroelectrics.

Abstract: The continued scaling of ferroelectric devices is critical for next-generation computing architectures, yet it is fundamentally challenged by a metrological bottleneck: at the deep sub-micrometer scale, intrinsic material properties are heavily masked by extrinsic parasitic impedances and geometric fringing fields. Here, we introduce a quantitative, in-situ nanoprobing framework capable of resolving the true electrical response of ferroelectric capacitors down to 165 nm in diameter without the need for lithographic bond pads. Using 20 nm thick AlScN as a model system, we establish a non-linear 'Screened Power Law' model to decouple attofarad-level device capacitances from massive near-field probe interactions. Furthermore, we demonstrate that the apparent degradation of dielectric loss at the nanoscale is a geometric dilution artifact, which we overcome through a conductance scaling analysis. Finally, we apply this framework to large-signal characterization, utilizing leakage-compensation and noise filtering protocols to extract pristine intrinsic hysteresis (C-V and J-E) loops in the discrete few-grain limit. These findings provide a universal analytical toolkit required to overcome the measurement limits of deep-submicron ferroelectrics.

</details>


### [127] [Emergent Fermi polarons in Dirac materials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04161)
*Xin Chen,Eugen Dizer,Rafał Ołdziejewski,Kostya S. Novoselov,Marton Kanász-Nagy,Richard Schmidt*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: This paper investigates band-structure effects on the absorption spectra of quantum impurities in Dirac materials, discovering novel Dirac-Fermi polarons that persist across various interaction regimes and doping levels, with spectroscopic signatures accessible in both solid-state and ultracold atomic platforms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the unique band structure of Dirac materials (with Dirac points) affects the absorption spectra of quantum impurities, and to reveal new quasiparticle physics beyond conventional approaches.

Method: Theoretical investigation of band-structure effects on impurity absorption spectra in Dirac materials, analyzing formation of quasiparticles from impurity dressing by excitations near the Dirac point.

Result: Discovery of Dirac-Fermi polarons — novel quasiparticles that are robust for both attractive and repulsive interactions, and across all electron/hole doping levels. Their spectroscopic signatures are generic features of Dirac materials.

Conclusion: Polaron spectroscopy serves as a powerful probe of Dirac points at energies far from the Fermi surface, enabling direct access to band-structure effects that are inaccessible with conventional methods.

Abstract: We investigate band-structure effects on the absorption spectra of quantum impurities in Dirac materials. We uncover the formation of novel quasiparticles -- Dirac-Fermi polarons -- emerging from the dressing of impurities by excitations near the Dirac point. These quasiparticles are remarkably robust, persisting for both attractive and repulsive interactions, and across the full range of electron and hole doping. We show that their spectroscopic signature is a generic feature of Dirac materials, accessible with established techniques in both solid-state and ultracold atomic platforms. Our results establish polaron spectroscopy as a powerful probe of Dirac points at energies far from the Fermi surface, providing direct access to band-structure effects beyond conventional approaches.

</details>


### [128] [Correlated-Electron Theory of Triplet-Triplet Multiexciton States in Polypentacene](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03706)
*Rupali Jindal,Alok Shukla,Sumit Mazumdar*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: Correlated-electron calculations of pentacene oligomers reveal that 1(T1T1) triplet-pair eigenstates are quantum superpositions of all accessible intertriplet separations within a narrow energy window near the lowest optical exciton, explaining the lack of intramolecular triplet diffusion while permitting intermolecular singlet fission.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the electronic structure of multiexciton states in pentacene oligomers as models for polypentacene, particularly to explain the apparent absence of intramolecular triplet diffusion and clarify the mechanism of singlet fission in these systems.

Method: Pariser-Parr-Pople Hamiltonian with multiple-reference singles and doubles configuration interaction; molecular exciton basis resolving Frenkel, charge-transfer, and triplet-pair (T1T1) configurations in real space; applied to three- and four-unit pentacene oligomers.

Result: All 1(T1T1) eigenstates reside in a narrow, nearly degenerate energy window near the lowest optical exciton. No single eigenstate maps to a localized triplet-pair configuration; rather, each is a quantum superposition across all accessible intertriplet separations.

Conclusion: The delocalized nature of triplet-pair eigenstates explains the lack of intramolecular triplet diffusion observed experimentally in pentacene oligomers and polymers, while still allowing intermolecular singlet fission in films with strong interchain interactions.

Abstract: We present correlated-electron calculations of optical spin-singlet and triplet-triplet multiexciton states in three- and four-unit pentacene oligomers as microscopic models for polypentacene. The calculations use the Pariser-Parr-Pople Hamiltonian, multiple-reference singles and doubles configuration interaction, and a molecular exciton basis that resolves Frenkel, charge-transfer, and triplet-pair (T1T1) configurations in real space. We find that the complete set of 1(T1T1) eigenstates lies in a narrow, nearly degenerate energy window near the lowest optical exciton and that no eigenstate can be identified with a single localized triplet-pair configuration. Instead, each triplet-pair eigenstate is a quantum superposition of configurations containing all accessible intertriplet separations. This electronic structure explains the perceived absence of intramolecular triplet diffusion in pentacene oligomers, polypentacene, and polytetracene solutions, while leaving open the possibility of intermolecular singlet fission in films with appreciable interchain interactions.

</details>


### [129] [Janus MgAlB_2 MBene: a dipole-engineered anode for ultrafast Li-ion transport and exceptional lithium storage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03823)
*Pritam Samanta,Sashank Kumar Pandey,Amit Kumar Jana,Prakash Parida*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: The paper proposes a Janus MBene, MgAlB₂, and uses first-principles calculations to show its excellent electrochemical properties for Li-ion battery anodes, including a low diffusion barrier (17.1 meV), high theoretical capacity (1470.24 mAh/g), and small volume expansion (3.7%), enabled by its intrinsic out-of-plane polarization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether intrinsic polarization in Janus 2D materials can be utilized to simultaneously improve Li-ion diffusion kinetics and storage capacity, overcoming limitations of symmetric parent monolayers (Mg₂B₂ and Al₂B₂) that support only a single Li adsorption layer.

Method: First-principles calculations (density functional theory) were performed to study the structural, electronic, dynamical/mechanical/thermal stability, and electrochemical properties of the Janus MgAlB₂ monolayer, including Li adsorption, diffusion barriers, charge analysis, and capacity/volume expansion upon lithiation.

Result: MgAlB₂ exhibits enhanced orbital hybridization near the Fermi level, excellent stability, an ultra-low Li diffusion barrier of 17.1 meV (room-temperature diffusion coefficient ~3.43×10⁻¹⁰ cm²/s), ability to accommodate two complete Li layers, a theoretical specific capacity of 1470.24 mAh/g, and a very small volume expansion of only 3.7% at maximum lithiation.

Conclusion: Intrinsic out-of-plane polarization in Janus monolayers can effectively regulate both the thermodynamics and kinetics of Li storage, offering a promising design strategy for high-rate and high-capacity 2D electrode materials.

Abstract: In this work, we propose a group II/IIIA-based Janus MBene, MgAlB_2, and investigate its electrochemical properties using first-principles calculations. The substitution of one Mg layer in Mg_2B_2 MBene by an Al layer breaks the structural symmetry and generates a permanent out-of-plane polarization, giving rise to a distinct electronic environment compared with the parent Mg_2B_2 and Al_2B_2 monolayers. Electronic-structure analysis reveals enhanced orbital hybridization among B, Mg, and Al states near the Fermi level, resulting in improved electronic delocalization across the monolayer. The Janus MgAlB_2 monolayer is found to possess excellent dynamical, mechanical, and thermal stability. Owing to its polarization-modified energy landscape, Li ions migrate with an exceptionally low diffusion barrier of 17.1 meV, corresponding to a room-temperature diffusion coefficient of 3.43x10^-10 cm^2/s. Unlike the pristine Mg_2B_2 and Al_2B_2 monolayers, which support only a single stable adsorption layer, MgAlB_2 accommodates two complete Li layers. Detailed analysis shows that the residual polarization retained after first-layer lithiation continues to promote Li adsorption, whereas increasing Li-Li electrostatic interactions eventually limit further storage. As a result, the Janus monolayer delivers a high theoretical specific capacity of 1470.24 mAh/g together with a small volume expansion of only 3.7% during maximum lithiation. The present study demonstrates that intrinsic polarization can be utilized to regulate both the thermodynamics and kinetics of Li storage, providing a design strategy for high-rate and high-capacity two-dimensional electrode materials.

</details>


### [130] [Morphology-Property Interplay in Chemo-Mechanics of Ion-Intercalation Active Particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03893)
*Rongyue Lin,Royal C. Ihuaenyi,Juner Zhu,Ruobing Bai*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: This study establishes a thermodynamically consistent single-particle framework to investigate how morphology, material properties, and mechanical constraints jointly govern lithiation and stress generation in ion-intercalation particles. Using analytical solutions and multiphysics simulations, the authors reveal that isotropic particles are sensitive to mechanical constraints, while transversely isotropic particles exhibit heterogeneous lithiation dominated by anisotropic diffusivity. Flux decomposition shows mechanical contributions to Li flux are negligible in spheres but dominant in ellipsoids, and Bayesian optimization identifies Pareto-optimal hollow ellipsoid morphologies balancing capacity and stress.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The coupled effects of morphology, material property, and mechanical constraint on chemo-mechanical behavior of ion-intercalation particles remain insufficiently understood, motivating the need for a unified framework to determine how these factors jointly regulate lithiation and stress generation.

Method: A thermodynamically consistent single-particle framework combining analytical solutions with multiphysics simulations. The study examines hollow spherical, cylindrical, and ellipsoidal particles with isotropic or transversely isotropic properties under fully constrained, inner-free, or unconstrained boundary conditions, using flux decomposition analysis, correlation analysis, and Bayesian optimization.

Result: Isotropic particles are sensitive to mechanical constraint, while transversely isotropic particles exhibit persistent heterogeneous lithiation dominated by anisotropic diffusivity. Mechanical contribution to Li flux is negligible in spheres but dominant in ellipsoids. Li concentration and volumetric strain show strong anti-correlation in unconstrained particles but weak correlation under full constraints. Bayesian optimization identified Pareto-optimal hollow ellipsoid morphologies balancing lithiation capacity and peak tensile stress.

Conclusion: The coupled interaction of morphology, property, and constraint governs chemo-mechanical behavior, not any single factor alone. The findings provide a unified framework for morphology-property interplay and offer morphology design principles for improving chemo-mechanical stability in intercalation particles.

Abstract: Morphology, material property, and mechanical constraint jointly govern the chemo-mechanical behavior of ion-intercalation particles, yet their coupled effects remain insufficiently understood. Here we establish a thermodynamically consistent single-particle framework and combine analytical solutions with multiphysics simulations to determine how these factors regulate lithiation and stress generation. We study hollow spherical, cylindrical, and ellipsoidal particles with isotropic or transversely isotropic material properties under fully constrained, inner-free, or unconstrained boundary conditions. We show that the transient lithiation pathway and the associated stress and strain fields are governed not by morphology, property, or constraint alone, but by their coupled interaction: isotropic particles are sensitive to the mechanical constraint, whereas transversely isotropic particles exhibit persistent heterogeneous lithiation dominated by anisotropic diffusivity. Flux decomposition analysis reveals that the mechanical contribution to Li flux is negligible in spheres but dominant in ellipsoids. Correlation analysis further shows that Li concentration and volumetric strain exhibit strong anti-correlation in unconstrained particles but weak correlation under full constraints. Bayesian optimization of hollow ellipsoids identifies Pareto-optimal morphologies that balance lithiation capacity against peak tensile stress. These results provide a unified framework for the morphology-property interplay in intercalation particles and offer morphology design principles for chemo-mechanical stability.

</details>


### [131] [First-principles study of the electronic structure and optical properties of two-dimensional $α$-graphdiyne](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03841)
*Ashkan Shekaari*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: The paper investigates monolayer α-graphdiyne using DFT, revealing a gapless Dirac semimetallic character with electronic states dominated by carbon 2p orbitals.
